# Decisions

What we chose, and why. Newest at the bottom.

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-28 | **Engine: Three.js with Vite** (from the handover) | Runs in the browser without installs, well known to AI tools, simple to host. |
| 2026-10-28 | **TypeScript instead of plain JavaScript** | The type checker catches mistakes on every build. That matters when nobody reads the code line by line. Three.js ships its own types. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Project folder outside the synced folders: `C:\Users\YoshiParlevliet\dev\briel-3d`** | A cloud-synced folder tries to sync every file. A web project has thousands of small files, and sync locks can corrupt git's history. The originals stay untouched where they were. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Backup via private GitHub repo** `yopazerbot/briel-GTA` | Off-site history of every step. Commits use the GitHub "noreply" address so no personal email ends up in the history. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Source copies stay out of git (`source\` is ignored)** | They are private documents and large. Only small derived data goes into the repo. |
| 2026-10-28 | **The main house's geometry comes from private files (Yoshi's choice)** | Yoshi named which files to use. They are not in the repository: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Floor heights of the main house: floors at 0, 3.05 and 5.88 m, ceilings at 2.70, 5.53 and 8.41 m** | Chosen once from the facade and section views. The numbers are in the public shell data. |
| 2026-10-28 | **The main house's walls were read by a tool, not traced by hand** | The tool and its inputs are in the private repository. The public shell keeps the outside only (see the private repository). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Hand-measured additions of the main house in one private file** | Every manual choice sits in one file with a note. It is not in the public repository: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Own simple collision instead of a physics library** | Every solid is a flat outline extruded between two heights. The player is a 25 cm radius cylinder that can step up 50 cm. It is about 150 lines of code and handles walls, glass, stairs and falling. It is also exact, because drawing and collision use the same blocks. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Sky drawn once into a texture; render at normal resolution** | The sky shader cost about half the frame time on the laptop's integrated graphics. With this change a frame takes 13 to 17 ms (60 to 75 fps) instead of about 42 ms. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Both control schemes always active; the start screen choice sets hints and pause behaviour** | You can pick up the controller at any time without restarting. "Keyboard + mouse" captures the mouse, "Controller" doesn't. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Nickname stored in the browser only** | A stopgap until the login system with user management exists. It will then come from the user account. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Python tools in a project-local environment** (`tools\.venv`) | Nothing is installed system-wide. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Temporary password gate on Railway** (HTTP Basic Auth in a small Node server, scrypt password hash, fail closed) | The custom domain makes the site public. The real login with user management and audit log comes later; this server is the start of that back end. |
| 2026-10-28 | **House data inside the web app (`web/src/data/house_shell.json`)** | Railway builds only the `web` folder, so the app must not read files outside it. The inside of the homes is sealed (see the rows of 6 November). |
| 2026-10-28 | **The main house follows Yoshi's newest choice** | A few manual edits to the outside (garden shed, claustra wall at the carport, outbuilding left out for a later phase). The tools are in the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Real textures (Poly Haven, CC0) and a photographed sky** | Biggest single step towards photorealism without a 3D artist. Downloads approved by Yoshi. Originals stay in `source/`; web copies (WebP, about 6 MB) are in git. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Facade brick generated, light beige** | No library brick matches; the size was taken from the site photos. Yoshi confirmed beige over the grey seen on overcast photos. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Graphics presets with Auto detection** (Low, Medium, High, Ultra) | This PC's graphics chip is low-end. Auto tests the real scene at start-up and picks the best preset that holds 60 fps; it steps down during play if needed. Choice is remembered per browser. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Real-time ambient occlusion now, Blender light bake next** | Soft corner shadows in real time are expensive on weak chips. A Blender bake stores light (including light bouncing between walls) in textures once, which looks better and runs faster. |
| 2026-10-28 | **No password on the live site, kept out of search engines** (Yoshi's choice, replaces the temporary password gate) | Simplest for family and friends. `noindex` header on every response, robots meta tag and `robots.txt`. Not access control: anyone with the URL can open it. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Interactive sliding doors (Yoshi's request)** | Panels are solid wherever they currently are, so a closed door blocks you. Which doors slide is part of the main house's inside (see the private repository). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Explore or Action mode, jumping, Minecraft-style flying, adjustable walking speed** | Yoshi's requests for a playful GTA feel. The pistol, hands and gun sounds are built in code (no downloads); bullet holes stay on the walls (last 80). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Claustra only in front of one window** | Corrected by Yoshi: the open brickwork is the outer brick layer in front of a window, not a wall across the carport. |
| 2026-10-28 | **The main house's fittings are blocks with real dimensions** | Built as solid blocks with real dimensions. They are part of the inside: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Multiplayer: up to 4 players, accounts, chat, admin** | Yoshi's request. One Node server (WebSocket via `ws`), Railway Postgres for accounts and the login log, admin password from a Railway variable, 3 player accounts made by the admin. Solo play stays open without login. Sessions in HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookies, origin checks on every API call and the WebSocket, login lockout per IP. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Build tools listed as regular dependencies** | Railway sets NODE_ENV=production; this keeps Vite and TypeScript installed for the build. |
| 2026-10-28 | **A wider sliding door; breakable windows** | Yoshi: the thin strip next to a sliding door is part of the opening. Windows are separate meshes so a shot can break one; the gap becomes walkable, broken windows are shared in multiplayer, "Repair windows" in the menu restores them. |
| 2026-10-28 | **One name field for solo and multiplayer; online list; visitor IP from Cloudflare** | A friend's login failed because the name went into the Nickname field and the separate username field stayed empty. Now "Your name" plus an optional password. Railway adds its own proxy hops to X-Forwarded-For, so the per-IP lockout used one shared Railway address for everyone; it now uses CF-Connecting-IP, but only when the address that connected to Railway (second X-Forwarded-For entry from the end, written by Railway's edge) is a Cloudflare server. Otherwise that address itself is used, so going around Cloudflare doesn't allow faking an IP. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Fighting rules: health 100, body 34, head 100, respawn 3 s, protection 2 s, first to 10 kills** | Yoshi asked for lives, respawn and points. "Lives" became health (respawning makes a fixed number of lives pointless). The shooter's browser reports hits and the server checks them (both armed and alive, not protected, at most one hit per 120 ms, within 120 m). Line of sight is not checked: fine among friends, not cheat-proof. Explore players can't be hurt. Scores survive a reconnect until the next round. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Sessions in the database; all-time stats** | A deploy no longer logs everyone out: sessions are stored as a SHA-256 hash of the token (a leaked database can't be used to log in). Kills, deaths, headshots and round wins per account are added up in the `stats` table; the pause menu shows the leaderboard. Deleting a user deletes their sessions and stats. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Stored sessions are checked again on start** | Security review: logout, kick, password reset and user deletion now wait for the database delete (errors reach the admin). On start, player sessions only come back for users that still exist; admin sessions only while ADMIN_PASSWORD is unchanged (compared with a scrypt fingerprint in the `meta` table). A login that overlaps with a kick or delete is undone. |
| 2026-10-28 | **3D sound and footsteps made in code; settings in the browser** | Web Audio HRTF panners place other players' shots, steps and breaking glass; the listener follows the camera. Footsteps are filtered noise per floor material (no audio files to download). One step per stride (about 0.85 m walking, 1.2 m running). Settings live in localStorage; turning slows down in proportion while zoomed in. Vibration uses the Gamepad API's dual-rumble where the browser supports it. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Weapons as pickups; numbers shared with the server** | Rifle 26 per body hit (4 hits), shotgun 8 pellets of 15 (30 to the head), sniper 100 body / 200 head, pistol 34. Stats in `web/shared/weapons.json`, read by both the browser and the server, so damage can't drift apart. The server only accepts hits from weapons you picked up, at that weapon's fire rate and pellet count. Pickups (`web/shared/pickups.json`) are granted by the server within 2.5 m. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Loading screen** | Yoshi saw the start screen flicker between the street and a room: those were the graphics test frames. A loading screen with a progress bar now covers loading and the test, then fades to the front of the house. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Server checks fire rate across weapons and movement speed** | Security review: a hit must come from the weapon in hand, with one shared next-shot time; position updates faster than 16 m/s are ignored (sprint is 10, a fall from the roof about 13), so pickups and hit range can't be reached by faking a teleport. Not checked: walls (a cheater could still pass through them at running speed). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Movement budget instead of a per-message limit** | Security review: the server keeps a movement budget per player that refills at 16 m/s (at most half a second stored), so sending many small updates can't add speed. A connection taken over from another device keeps its position; new players start on the street. Still accepted as a known limit: positions come from the browser and walls are not checked on the server, so a determined cheater can move through walls at running speed. Fine among friends; a server-side collision check would be the next step if needed. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Other players: the SWAT character by Quaternius (CC0)** | Yoshi asked for real character models. The Toon Shooter kit characters are chibi-style; the SWAT model has realistic proportions, tactical gear and 24 animations (idle, walk, run, strafe, run back, shoot, death) at 1.5 MB without textures. Recoloured per player (olive, brown, desert, black) with a scarf in the player's colour. The gun is placed in the right hand and points where the player looks; the upper body leans slightly. No crouch animation, so crouching presses the figure down. The block figures remain as a fallback if the model can't load. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Gun models: Quaternius CC0** | Yoshi asked for free-to-use guns. Four CC0 models (pistol, assault rifle, pump shotgun, sniper rifle, 40 to 130 KB each) replace the block guns in your hands, on the floor and in other players' hands. CC-BY models were skipped to avoid attribution strings in the game. The block guns stay as a fallback; with the models, reloading is a tilt of the gun (the magazine/pump/bolt parts belonged to the block guns). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Light bake: pipeline done, still a test setting** | `tools/bake/`: the game exports its static house meshes, Blender (Cycles, CPU) bakes sun, sky and bounced light into one 2048 texture (about 4 minutes at 512 samples), and the game draws the house as texture x baked light without real-time lighting. Outside surfaces stay real-time (baking them spent the texture on the street). Lessons: the game's faces are double-sided, so Blender must turn them outward; three.js divides a basic material's light map by pi; real indoor light is about 12 times darker than sunlit outside, so the texture stores the square root (more steps in the dark) and the eyes adapt 6 times indoors. Still noisy and dark in places, so it is a setting ("Baked lighting (test)") and real-time lighting stays the default. Next: denoise the bake (OIDN), and light probes for people and furniture. |
| 2026-10-28 | **An exercise bike in the house (Yoshi's choice)** | A stationary bike with a screen, part of the starting furniture. The layout is private: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Starting furniture rechecked against the reference images** | Yoshi found the first layout did not follow the reference images, so every position was checked view by view. Six pieces were added to the catalogue. The server replaces an older starting layout only if nobody has placed, moved or removed furniture since. The layout is part of the private inside (see the private repository). |
| 2026-10-28 | **Starting furniture from the reference images (replaced, see above)** | Yoshi asked to furnish the house after the reference images. The server loads the layout once into an empty database; the admin (or anyone playing alone) can go back to it with a menu button. The layout is private: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Furniture planner with furniture made in code** | Free furniture models were thin on Poly Pizza (CC0) and Kenney's site asks not to be scraped by AI tools, so the 25 pieces are built from simple shapes at real sizes (bed 160 x 200, sofa 220 x 90): exact sizes matter most for planning. One shared material and one instanced draw per kind keeps a furnished house light on weak graphics. Shared through the server (table `furniture`) when logged in, localStorage otherwise. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Surroundings from Flemish open data** | Yoshi asked to use OpenStreetMap or Flemish government data for the neighbours. OSM only has footprints from the address register; the GRB (Digitaal Vlaanderen) has plot lines, buildings and road surface, and DHM Vlaanderen II (laser scan, 1 m) gives heights. The real plot (1061/D0) is 15 x 40 m: the back hedge moved 8.5 m back and the side hedges onto the plot line. Neighbouring buildings get walls to the measured eaves and a gable roof to the measured ridge; trees stand where the scan found tall vegetation (made in code, no downloads). The placeholder street is replaced by the real lane; the start position is in the middle of it. The laser scan is from 2013-2015: our house and the one being built next to it are not in it. To keep the exact location out of URLs, data was requested with the area in the request body, or as a whole 1 km tile. |
| 2026-10-28 | **House rules for the admin: flying and moving furniture** (Yoshi's request) | Players flew around and rearranged the furniture during a game. The settings now have two admin-only switches for multiplayer: allow flying, and let players move furniture. The server keeps them in the database (meta `rules`), tells everyone when they change and enforces them: while flying is off nobody is shown flying, and while furniture is off it ignores furniture changes from everyone except the admin, who can still arrange and reset the house. Playing alone, both are always allowed. |
| 2026-10-28 | **The bungalow moved to its real plot down the lane, with an upper floor** (Yoshi's request) | Yoshi marked its real place on the map: the GRB outline of the plot Yoshi marked, about 190 m from the plot. The house (9.20 x 14.00 m) stands in that outline (12.7 x 21.1 m: the house grew later), centred, front to the road; the garage on the 4.1 x 9.9 m outline beside it (both GRB outlines left out). The surroundings now reach 230 m from the plot (buildings, the road, more trees), so the road and neighbours are there too; the server accepts positions up to 300 m. The upper floor and its stair are part of the inside: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **The admin's bazooka** (Yoshi's request) | Key 5, for the admin in multiplayer and for anyone playing alone. One rocket per load (2.8 s reload), flies at 32 m/s up to 150 m to whatever is under the crosshair. The server sets off the blast when the rocket arrives and decides the damage itself from the positions it knows: up to 160 at the centre, nothing beyond 5 m, the shooter spared, walls do not shield. Everyone sees the rocket and the explosion; the shooter's blast also breaks windows within 3.5 m and fells cats. The sight sits on the left of the tube, so aiming looks past it. |
| 2026-10-28 | **An old bungalow down the lane** (Yoshi's request) | Built in Blender: single storey, 9.20 x 14.00 m, tiled gable roof with chimney, pale brick on a dark plinth, double casement windows with stone surrounds, front door with fanlight and steps on the long side, and a 4 x 10 m flat-roofed garage. The arched fanlight is square. It stands on the empty field west of the plot, north of the lane, front gable to the lane, with a path and a drive. Walk-in. Own materials (`old_*`), left out of the light bake, so the bake of Yoshi's house stays valid. Code: in the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Admin switch: names above players** (Yoshi's request) | The names float above players and show through walls, so they work as a wallhack. A third house rule turns them off for everyone. Note: the minimap still shows other players as dots. |
| 2026-10-28 | **No bullet tracers** (Yoshi's request) | With several players shooting, the streaks filled the screen. Shots keep their muzzle flash, sound and impact effects. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Windows and outside doors with their own panes** | Yoshi pointed out that some windows are single panes. Each window now has its own panes instead of a mullion every 1.5 m. Sashes that open get a second frame. Frames are RAL 8017 brown outside and RAL 9010 white inside. Corner windows are glass to glass, without a corner post. Some glass is frosted. The per-window numbers are part of the main house's data: see the private repository. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Aim with each gun model's own sights** | Yoshi found the rifle sights looked wrong. The aiming position was tuned for the first block guns, but the CC0 models have higher sights. It is now measured from each model when it loads: the top of the front sight, or the middle of the sniper scope, on the centre of the view. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Window frames and inner doors** (frames replaced, see above) | Grey/brown aluminium frames (5 cm face, 7 cm deep) around every window, with mullions at most 1.5 m apart, also along the large glass walls. Every door opening up to 1.25 m wide without a door gets a swinging leaf: inside doors stand open and swing into the room (not into a hall), outside doors start closed. Hinge side is a guess. Open and close with E/click like the sliding doors; shared in multiplayer. |
| 2026-10-28 | **Cats** | Yoshi asked for cat NPCs to shoot. Four animated cats (Quaternius, CC0) walk fixed loops in the garden, front lawn and side path on a clock shared with the server, so everyone sees them in the same place without position traffic. Shot: a yowl, a puff of fur (no blood, cartoon style), the death animation, back after 15 s. No points; the kill feed shows it. Loops in `web/shared/cats.json`. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Effects drawn in code: soft particles, muzzle flashes, bullet holes per surface, casings** | Yoshi asked for better-looking guns and effects without making performance harder. Explosions, smoke and dust were square points, the flash a yellow sphere and holes black circles. Now one sprite sheet drawn on a canvas at start-up (no downloads) gives soft smoke, glows, chips and wisps. All particles are two instanced draw calls in fixed pools, so a shot creates no new objects and causes no hitches. The rocket blast has a flash, a fireball, rolling smoke, debris, embers, a ring of dust and a scorch mark, and shakes the view for a moment instead of nudging your aim. Holes have a look per surface and are one draw call for all of them (up to 80 meshes before). Casings fly out to the right and tinkle; shotgun shells come out with the pump, sniper cases with the bolt. On Low, big smoke puffs are halved: this laptop is limited by pixels, not objects. Tracers stay off (Yoshi's earlier decision). |
| 2026-10-29 | **The gun moves like it has weight** | It lags behind turning, tilts when you step sideways, dips on landing, is carried low while sprinting, springs back after each shot with a little roll and rocks when the pump or bolt is worked. Done on the processor, so it costs no frame time. The CC0 gun models came nearly black and half metallic; they now get real metal and wood finishes, so they reflect the sky. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Other players hold their gun with both hands** | The SWAT clips hold every gun in one outstretched hand, and the 'Gun_Shoot' aiming frame turns the body side-on and bows the head. The arms are now bent onto the gun after the animation (two-bone IK): long guns in the right shoulder, the pistol held out in front. The model's arms are short, so long guns are drawn at 85% in their hands and the chest turns a little to let the left hand reach. Standing armed uses 'Idle_Gun_Pointing' (upright); walking slowly uses 'Walk'. A shot pushes the gun back and the hands go with it. When your bullet hits someone they flinch: the model's own hit clip ('HitRecieve', unused until now), played on top of whatever they are doing. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Neighbours' facades made up in the style of the street** (`tools/facades.py`) | The open data gives each building's outline and heights only. Now each house gets a brick (red, red-brown, yellow, white-painted or grey, standard 21 x 5 cm, generated), roof tiles (red clay, orange, anthracite, black or brown, generated with rolls and overlaps) and window frames, fixed by its position so it never changes between runs. Windows and doors follow simple rules: the wall facing the road (found from the road outline) gets the front door and wider windows, the back wider windows and a garden door, the sides fewer; an upper row on tall houses, gable windows under high roofs; outbuildings facing the road get a garage door. Walls against a neighbour (terraced houses) stay blind. Glass is opaque, dark and glossy: the houses have no rooms behind it. Frames and doors are one draw call, glass another. They are not the real facades; real colours for the nearest houses can be set later if Yoshi wants. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Hedges and fences on the real plot lines** | GRB plot outlines (already downloaded, unused until now) give the lines between gardens. Only plots with a building, only within 35 m of a building (deep plots end in meadow), not along the road and not around our plot. Per boundary a clipped hedge (50%), a dark green welded wire fence on concrete posts (35%) or nothing; lower within 7 m of the road. They are solid. 3.4 km in all, so no leafy lumps on these hedges (that would be over a million triangles). |
| 2026-10-29 | **Concrete edge strip along the road; canopies; walls to the ground** | A 35 cm light concrete strip along both edges of the asphalt instead of asphalt running into the grass (common on lanes like this; to be checked by Yoshi). The eight GRB canopies (afdak) are flat roofs on posts. Neighbours' walls now reach below our flat ground: where the measured terrain was higher they floated above the grass. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Shadows on the ground of the whole neighbourhood, worked out once** | The real-time sun shadow only covers about 17 m around the house, so the street had no shadows at all. When the sky has loaded, the static scene is rendered twice into depth only (from the sun and from straight above) and combined into one ground texture: the sun's shadow of every house, hedge and tree, a soft darkening where walls meet the ground and under trees, and a slow colour variation that breaks up the repeating grass. Grass and road read it once per pixel; near the house the real-time shadow takes over. Grass seen towards the sun reflected the sky like sand; that reflection is turned down on grass. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Haze and a horizon** | The ground ended in a hard line 400 m out against the sky. Far things now fade into the colour of the sky just above the horizon (taken from the sky photo), and a ring of distant tree lines, hedgerows and farm roofs, drawn on a canvas, stands 420 m out. Measured on Low (headless, same window, median of three): outdoor views 5.5 to 5.8 ms before, 5.7 to 6.0 ms after; inside unchanged. 424 draw calls instead of 392. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Grenades: two each, the thrower's browser flies them, the server hurts** | G or R1. Everyone carries two; a thrown one is back after 15 s; 3 s fuse; 4.5 m blast, 130 at the centre (`web/shared/grenade.json`, read by both sides). The flight needs the house's collision blocks, which only the browsers have, so the thrower's browser flies it (4 cm steps so it can't pass through a wall or pane, a third of the speed bounces back, it rolls to a stop) and tells the server where it went off. The others fly the same throw on their screen and swap it for the blast when it arrives. The thrower's browser also says who stood behind a wall or floor (checked every 10 cm along the line to their chest); the server spares them. A browser can only spare people that way, never hurt more. The server counts each player's grenades and only accepts a blast 2 to 6 s after one of their throws, within 60 m of it. The thrower is spared, as with the bazooka. A hard throw breaks a window and flies on. R1 used to be next weapon (D-pad up still is); throwing is on R1 as in most shooters, and R1 still changes the piece in the furniture planner. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Faster loading: brotli for static files only, ETags, the light bake on demand** | Measured in a headless browser: the live site downloaded 15.9 MB on a first visit and 8.6 MB on a revisit (no ETags and `no-cache`, so nothing could be reused; Cloudflare does not compress the sky photo or the models). Now 9.9 MB and 0.04 MB. The build writes a brotli copy (.br) next to files it shrinks by at least a tenth (sky 5.5 to 2.9 MB, character model 1.6 to 0.25 MB); the server sends it when the browser accepts brotli. Compression side channels (BREACH) need a secret and visitor input in the same compressed answer: only static files, the same for everyone, are compressed; API answers carry `no-transform` so Cloudflare does not compress them either (it did by default for JSON); the WebSocket has compression off; the server never decompresses anything. The baked lighting (1.7 MB) downloaded at every start although it is a test setting; now only when it is switched on. Splitting the planner and admin code out of the script was dropped: together they are 8 KB. |
| 2026-10-29 | **The texture warning at start-up** | "Texture marked for update but no image data found" came 186 times in the first 0.2 s: setting the texture sharpness for the graphics preset flagged textures that were still downloading. Now only loaded ones are flagged; the others get the setting when they arrive. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Local server on Windows: retry replacing its data file** | Without a database the local server keeps users in `web/.data/store.json`. On this PC replacing that file failed right after writing it (the virus scanner holds it for a moment), so the local server did not start. It now tries again for up to a second. Railway uses Postgres and is not affected. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Password guessing: per IPv6 /64, per account, two checks at a time** | The limit of 10 wrong passwords per 15 minutes counted per full address. An IPv6 connection usually gets a whole /64, so the limit did not hold. There was no limit per account. Every guess also ran scrypt (16 MB) on Node's four worker threads, which also read the files the site sends, so a flood slowed the whole site. Now IPv6 counts per /64, and an account takes 20 wrong passwords an hour. That limit counts any name, so it doesn't reveal which accounts exist. A browser that logged into the account in the last 90 days has a signed device cookie (OWASP's answer to lockouts) and still gets in, so guessing can't lock the admin out. At most 2 password checks run at a time and 8 wait; more are answered busy. Attempts are now counted in place: before, simultaneous guesses each saved an outdated copy of the count. Tested locally against the server in production mode (33 checks). |
| 2026-10-29 | **Only through Cloudflare** | Railway also answers for huisje.yoshiparlevliet.be when someone connects to its edge directly (tested 2026-10-29). That skips Cloudflare's rules and its minimum TLS version. With `TRUST_CLOUDFLARE` the server now refuses requests whose connecting address (from Railway's X-Forwarded-For) is not a Cloudflare address. `/healthz` stays open for Railway's own check. The list in `web/server/auth.mjs` matches cloudflare.com/ips on 2026-10-29; when Cloudflare adds ranges it needs an update, or visitors coming through the new ranges get 403. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Login log: 90 days, at most 20,000 entries** | In Postgres every failed login added a row with the IP address and the typed name, and nothing removed them. IP addresses are personal data, which GDPR says to keep no longer than needed, and a flood of guesses could grow the table without limit. Old entries are removed at start and every hour. The local file already kept only the last 500. |
| 2026-10-29 | **`__Host-` cookies and three more headers** | `__Host-briel_session` and `__Host-briel_device` can only be set by this host over HTTPS, so another subdomain of yoshiparlevliet.be can't plant one (cookie tossing). Everyone logs in once more after the change. Locally (plain HTTP) the cookies keep their names without the prefix. Permissions-Policy turns off camera, microphone, location, payment and USB, which the site doesn't use. Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy and Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy `same-origin` keep other sites from opening the pages or embedding the files. |
| 2026-10-29 | **The Tesla: a Model Y built in Blender** | Yoshi asked for a drivable Tesla Model Y on the driveway, from a licence-free 3D model, shown in multiplayer, in black. No CC0 Model Y exists (Sketchfab, Poly Pizza and the shops checked): the free ones are CC-BY, often uploaded by people who don't own them, and Sketchfab needs a login to download. A CC0 SUV (Quaternius) was tried and replaced when Yoshi asked for a more realistic one made in Blender from photos. `tools/model_y.py` lofts the body from cross-sections along the length (side profile, width and window line from the published sizes 4.79 x 1.92 x 1.62 m, wheelbase 2.89 m, 19-inch wheels), checked against photos of the 2025 car on Wikimedia Commons (used only to compare, not shipped). It cuts the wheel arches and lays the front light bar, headlights, smoked rear light bar and mirrors on the surface: about 20,000 triangles, 0.09 MB with brotli. It is our own model, so no licence or credit is needed. |
| 2026-10-29 | **Driving: camera behind the car, simple physics, the server keeps the car** | A camera behind the car (GTA-like) shows where you are going better than a view from a seat without an interior. The physics is arcade: speed along the car, sideways sliding that dies out (the handbrake lets it slide), steering that turns less sharply at speed, top speed about 80 km/h. The car is a box against the house blocks and a circle per player and tree trunk; it slides along walls and bumps back when it hits head-on. Yoshi chose that it can't hurt players: they just stop it. In multiplayer the server keeps one car and its driver; the driver's position updates are the car's, so the server's speed check allows car speed (30 m/s) for that player only. Getting in is checked on the server (nobody else driving, alive, next to the car). The driver can't be shot or shoot. |
| 2026-10-30 | **Multiplayer with a shared PIN** | Yoshi asked for solo or multiplayer without a password per player, with one PIN for everyone (0473, hardcoded is fine). The PIN is only checked on the server (never in the page's code), from `MULTIPLAYER_PIN` with 0473 as the default. A 4-digit PIN has 10,000 values, so it is guessable without limits: wrong PINs count against the address (10 in 15 minutes, as wrong passwords) and against everyone together (30 an hour, about a week of guessing for an even chance); browsers that joined before carry a signed device cookie and are never held back by that last limit, so a guesser can't lock the family out. Guests can't take the name of an account (the admin's), nor a name another guest joined with while that session lasts (12 hours, or until logout), so nobody can play or score under someone else's name; can't use the admin's tools, and their sessions end when an account with that name is made. Kicking a guest also blocks their address for PIN joins for the 10 minutes, so they can't come straight back under another name (an account can still log in from there), and the admin can switch PIN joining off in the settings. Accounts and the admin's password login stay. A longer PIN in `MULTIPLAYER_PIN` would make guessing much slower. |
| 2026-10-30 | **The house's colours from the reference images** | The walls looked grey: the plaster texture was graded to a mid grey, and a tint can only darken. White paint is about 80 % reflectance, the texture was 25 %. The textures are now at the reference images' levels (light plaster, paler brick with sandy ochre joints in a wild bond, slatted cladding), and the indoor brightening is less (1.05 instead of 1.35). Dark aluminium copings on the roof edges and concrete caps on the terrace walls give the reference images' crisp top lines. |
| 2026-10-30 | **Furniture and fittings made in Blender** | The office chair (an Aeron look-alike), the coffee machine (Jura E8 look-alike in piano black), the soundbar (Sonos Arc look-alike, white) and the wall-hung toilet are our own models from published sizes and photos, so no licence or credit is needed, and no logos. Furniture models become coloured parts like the other furniture (one draw per kind); the chair's mesh is see-through up close. Fixed fittings (coffee machine, toilets) keep their own materials. Screens and light strips are small separate meshes; the bike's screen is redrawn 15 times a second only when someone is within 12 m. |

The glass in door leaves gets numbers from 3000 (after the house's own blocks), so breaking, the multiplayer sync and "Repair windows" work as for windows; the server accepts numbers below 4000 (it still keeps at most 200 broken). A leaf without its glass no longer blocks the way. |

The menu uses three columns so it fits a 1280 x 720 screen without scrolling. |

| 2026-10-30 | **The admin chooses how many may play** | Yoshi asked to raise the number of players in the game. It is a house rule like flying: 4 to start, the admin can set 2 to 16 in the settings, kept across restarts. 16 is the cap because every player's position goes to every other player ten times a second. The server checks the value and only the admin may set it; lowering it doesn't throw anyone out, it only stops new joins. Player colours go up to 16. |
| 2026-10-30 | **Neighbours' roofs from the laser scan and the aerial photo** | Yoshi asked for better shapes of the surrounding homes from open data. The 3D GRB of Digitaal Vlaanderen is only a block model with flat roofs (LOD1; roof shapes exist for Antwerp only), and the newer laser scan (DHMV III) isn't offered as a web service, so the 2013-2015 scan (1 m) stays the height source but is used better: each footprint is cut into parts (at inside corners and wherever two pieces fit clearly better than one, as a gabled house with a flat-roofed annex) and each part gets the roof shape that fits its heights best (flat, gable along or across, hip, lean-to; simpler shapes win unless a harder one fits clearly better), with its own eaves and ridge. Before, every house had one gable along its long side at one height. The most recent orthophoto (25 cm) gives each roof its colour (haze taken out, solar panels towards anthracite, grouped into eight colours so the roofs stay a few draw calls) and shows the 16 buildings the scan missed (built since): they get usual heights and, where the photo shows it, their shape. A check image (data/debug/roofs_on_photo.jpg) draws the fitted ridges on the photo. |
| 2026-10-30 | **The admin changes the PIN in the game** | Yoshi asked to change the PIN as admin in the settings. It is kept in the database (MULTIPLAYER_PIN, or 0473, is only the PIN to start with) and only the admin can see or change it (4 to 12 digits; longer is harder to guess). A new PIN logs out everyone who joined with the old one, starts the shared count of wrong PINs afresh, and ends the right of browsers that joined with the old PIN to skip that count (the device cookie is signed for the PIN it was earned with). The PIN is never written to the log. |
| 2026-10-30 | **The admin logs in under any name** | Yoshi asked to log in as admin with any name, checking only the password. A name that is an account's still needs that account's password (the admin password doesn't open it), and the admin can't take a name a guest is using. Tries at the admin password (any name that isn't an account) count against one shared limit (`#admin`): otherwise someone guessing it could pick a new name for every try. Once that limit is used up the admin password is refused like a wrong one (except from the admin's own browser, by its device cookie), so the answer gives nothing away. Every name, account or not, also has the same limit of its own, so the answers never tell which names are accounts (a security review caught a first version where they did). Attempts are counted before the account lookup, so parallel tries can't all slip under a limit. |
| 2026-10-30 | **Party games, kept by the server** | Hide and seek, tag and a treasure hunt, started and stopped by the admin. The server picks the seeker or who is "it", runs the clock, places the coins (in the rooms of the house and a few spots outside) and checks every tag and every coin against where the players are, so a modified browser can't tag from across the house or collect coins remotely. No hits, rockets or grenades are accepted while a game runs. In hide and seek names over heads and the dots on the minimap are off, and the seeker's screen is black while the others hide. |

| 2026-10-30 | **Riding the horses** | Yoshi asked for the horses to be rideable. E next to one gets you on: W and S (or R2 and L2) to walk, trot and stop, Shift (L3) to gallop, A and D to steer, seen from the saddle; you may shoot from horseback. The herd's walks are shared data (`web/shared/horses.json`) so the server can tell where a horse is that nobody rides and only lets a player on who stands next to it. The server keeps who rides which horse; the rider's updates move it (up to a gallop), and a horse that was ridden stays where it was left and grazes there until a restart. Its legs follow its speed: a walk in four beats, a trot in diagonal pairs, a gallop, blended into each other; the lower legs fold as they swing forward (when the model has knee and hock joints). The body bounces at a trot and rocks at a gallop, the horse leans into turns, and the view from the saddle moves with it. A gallop uses breath (about 9 s from full; it trots until it has some back) and Space jumps 1.5 m, enough for the hedges; hooves are heard as they land (sharper on paving), with now and then a snort, an ear flick or a shake of the head. |
| 2026-10-30 | **The neighbours' facades from Street View** | With the Claude extension in Yoshi's Edge, Street View could be used (Google refused it to the automated browser). A survey of the 89 houses within 80 m of a road, from the road in front of each: 75 could be described (images from 2023 for 49 of them, from 2009 for the rest); 6 are blurred at the owners' request and were left so, 8 aren't visible from any road with imagery. `data/facades.json` keeps per house, by its middle in the model (no addresses, no images, no notes): the wall material (red, red-brown, beige or yellow, white-painted or rendered, grey, or a new dark anthracite brick), the frames, the front door, an integrated garage door and its side, a chimney, a darker plinth. `tools/facades.py` uses these instead of the made-up look (outbuildings next to a house take its walls and frames), puts the garage door in the front wall where Street View showed it, and the build adds the chimney on the main ridge and the plinth band. Windows stay placed by the generator; dormers and window counts aren't modelled yet. |
| 2026-10-30 | **The real relief** | Yoshi asked for the real heights of the street and the surroundings. The laser scan's terrain (DHM II, 1 m) rises and falls by 9 m within the 540 m ring (Briel itself climbs at most 3.8 %), but within 50 m of the plot by less than a metre. `tools/terrain.py` turns it into a grid every 4 m (each point the mean of its square, so field ridges and narrow ditches don't show), relative to the plot's ground; the plot and the old bungalow keep a flat area at the model's ground (45 and 35 m out, blending into the real slopes over the next 40 to 50 m), because the house, the garden, the bungalow and everything placed there were built on flat ground. Between the points the ground is two flat triangles per square, and one shared formula (shared/terrain.mjs, the same in Python) gives that surface to the build, the browser and the server. The ground is drawn from the grid out to 560 m, with a skirt to the horizon and the dips (wadi, ponds, the ditch) cut out exactly; collision, walking, driving, riding, grenades and the strongmen follow it. Houses, pools, canopies, poles and trees are lifted whole to the ground at their middle (walls reach below the lowest corner); roads, yards, fields, tracks, hedges and fences are stored whole with a piece size, and the browser cuts their triangles along the grid and lifts every corner (the data stays near 1 MB). The server's map of where strongmen can walk measures blocks from the ground under them. Cost: about 1.5 ms a frame on Medium against the flat lawn, measured in the same session. |
| 2026-10-30 | **House against bungalow** | Yoshi asked for a mode with two teams, the bungalow and the main house, each spawning at its own. The admin starts it from the Admin tab like the party games, but it is a fight: the server puts everyone on the smaller team (in random order, so the teams are as even as can be), and players switch in the menu's Together tab; a switch starts you again at your new team's house without counting as a death, and someone who joins during the game goes to the smaller team. Spawn points: the 13 round the house for the house team (and for everyone outside the teams game), eight new ones at the bungalow for its team. The server ignores shots, rockets and grenades on your own team (the browser doesn't even send them); a kill of the other team scores for the killer's team, the first team to 25 kills wins, or the better score after 10 minutes. The personal first-to-10 round doesn't end the game meanwhile. Everyone wears their team's colour (blue for the house, orange for the bungalow: the outfit, the name, the dot on the map) until the game ends. A security review then caught two flaws, fixed the same day: a team name such as "constructor" passed the check (it is a property every JavaScript object has), left the player without spawn points and crashed the server from one message; and switching teams revived a dead player at once with full health. Team names are now checked against the two real ones, a respawn falls back to every spawn point, and every message from a browser is handled inside an error guard, so a mistake is logged instead of stopping the server. A switch while waiting to respawn takes effect at the respawn; while alive only at full health and once in 15 seconds. Tested again (15 checks, three runs). |

Trees: up to 1700 (every tree near the plot, the tallest out to 240 m, one per 7 m in the woods beyond); those over 160 m away get a simpler crown (80 triangles instead of about 600) and throw no live shadow. The lawn is drawn after what lies on it and a little behind in depth, so a field a few millimetres above it doesn't flicker far off. The ground reaches 900 m and the painted horizon stands there. Cost, measured headless: Low about 1.5 ms more a frame (27 to 28 ms), Medium about 5 ms more seen from the air over the whole ring (37 ms), spread over trees, fields and walls; the page's code with the house data is 441 KB compressed. |

What he does to players goes through the same health as the fighting, but nobody scores for it. |

| 2026-10-30 | **Ready-made half-size textures for phones, and a diagnostics panel** | Yoshi's iPhone showed the sky but every surface black. It doesn't happen here: headless Chromium and WebKit on this PC both draw the scene, and the 3D view wasn't lost (the lost-view message didn't show). The one step only phones took was shrinking every texture through a 2D canvas before upload. Phones now download half-size files made beforehand instead (`tools/textures.py --halves`, 1.9 MB, also a quarter of the download). Opening the site with `?diag` shows the graphics chip, the preset, the graphics memory, WebGL errors, a lost view, failed files and script errors on screen; `?diag&plain` also drops the textures, to tell a texture fault from a lighting one. Nothing leaves the device. |
| 2026-10-30 | **Full screen on phones and tablets** | Yoshi asked for it. Android and iPad get the browser's full screen from a button (turned sideways where the browser allows it). Safari on an iPhone has no full screen for pages, so there the button explains Add to Home Screen. A web app manifest (full screen, landscape) and Apple's tags make the home-screen icon open without Safari's bars. The HUD and touch buttons keep clear of the notch (safe-area insets). |
| 2026-10-30 | **The sky photo's sun capped under the half-precision limit** | The iPhone still drew every surface black. Its `?diag` panel showed WebGL 2 with every feature, all 76 textures loaded, no WebGL errors and no script errors, so the surfaces were computed black. The sky's light maps are half-precision floats, which end at 65504. The sun in the sky photo reaches 73216 (two pixels). This PC's graphics store such a value as 65504 (tested), while an iPhone stores infinity. Blurring the maps spreads it into every reflection and the result is black, while the sky itself still draws. The photo is now capped at 60000 before the maps are made; the sunlight comes from the directional light, so nothing visible changes here. |
| 2026-10-30 | **A rendered still behind the start menu** | Yoshi asked for a high-quality render for the start screen. It is the start view, path-traced in Blender (Cycles) from the game's own scene. The scene is exported from the game together with its camera, sun and the sky's turn (`tools/still/export_scene.js`). Blender re-attaches the game's textures with the game's tiling, makes real glass of the window panes and lights everything with the same sky photo at 8k, whose sun gives the sunlight and shadows (`tools/still/render_still.py`). The game's haze is added per material. 2560 x 1440 at 384 samples takes about 8 minutes on half the cores; the web gets 2560 and 1280 wide WebP (385 and 112 KB), and the page picks by screen width. While the start menu is up the picture covers the live view. |

It is a blue line on the big map and on the minimap, and the distance shown is the distance by road. When going straight there is as short (across the garden) or the road way is more than three times as long (across a field), the line goes straight. Hide and seek hides the other players on it as on the minimap. |

The processor works out where each animal is and what it does (43 sets of numbers a frame); the vertex shader turns the legs in a four-beat walk, the neck and head down to the grass, the tail now and then, and folds the legs lying down. The coat is worked out per pixel from the animal's own number: black and white, red and white, the blue roan of a Belgian Blue, a cream fleece with a white or a black face. A soft shadow lies under each one. Within 25 m of you each is a block you can't walk through. The nearest one moos or bleats now and then (made in code). |

| 2026-10-31 | **Traffic on the roads, on the shared clock** | Yoshi asked for more life along the road to the village, with good performance. Six vehicles drive the paved roads (one carriageway with a fixed surface in the GRB's Wegsegment, which also has the street names). Two go round the Briel loop and past our house. Four cross the village and drive on 150 m past the edge of the map along the real road, where the haze hides them, then come back the other way after 40 s: in along Kapellenhoek and out along Molenhoek (a car and a white van), Schildekensstraat to Stefaan Glorieuxstraat through the centre, and north to south down Kapellenhoek. `tools/roads.py` picks each route by the junctions it passes, keeps it 1.3 m right of the centre line, rounds the corners and gives every point its highest speed: 50 km/h on the open road, 30 within 200 m of the church, 25 at a junction, less in a bend (at most 1.8 m/s² sideways). The browser turns that into a timetable (1.5 m/s² to speed up, 2.2 to slow down) and puts each car on it by the server's clock, like the cats and the horses: every player sees the same car at the same spot. Nothing new is sent and the server doesn't know about the cars, so there is no new message to validate and nothing a client could forge or flood. A car stops 2 m short of anyone standing or driving in its lane (each browser for what it sees), honks after 3 s and every 10 s after, then drives up to a quarter faster until it is back on its timetable; a car that fell far behind jumps back only out of sight. Two cars of the traffic that block each other at a junction: the lower number goes. The cars move by the clock, not the frame time, in steps of at most 0.1 s, so a slow device or a hitch doesn't carry them past what they stop for. Within 90 m of the eye a car is a moving block for walking and driving into; bullets spark off it and their holes go with it. Within 70 m its engine and tyres are heard (a plain stereo panner: the game's HRTF one costs more for a sound that plays all the time). Brake lights come on when it slows, the body pitches with the slope, the wheels roll and the front ones steer. The models are our own (`tools/traffic_cars.py`): a compact hatchback, an estate and a panel van at the usual sizes of their class, no make or badge, about 900 triangles each, a 300-triangle copy for further off (60 m), one 96-triangle wheel. | 2026-10-31 | **brieltje.be/interested: who wants to play together in person, and their house in the game** | Yoshi wants to poll the community for a meetup and offer to build people's houses into the game. A page of its own (`web/public/interested/`), Dutch or English, in two short steps. Step 1: name and email (required), the street with suggestions from the Flemish address register (looked up by our server, so the visitor's typing doesn't go to a third party under their address), when it would suit them (weekday evening, Saturday, Sunday), how many would come, comments. Step 2, optional: plans of their home (a PDF or images: JPG, PNG, HEIC, WebP or TIFF, so a photo of a paper plan or a scan will do; up to 10 files of 25 MB) with three explicit consents, all unticked by default: they live there or have the owner's permission and may share the plans; the 3D model of their house becomes public in the game and in the public source code; the plans stay private and consent can be withdrawn (copies of the public code can't be recalled). Yoshi chose: only the model is public, the plans never; no email check for now; kept until withdrawn. The plans go to a private Railway bucket in Amsterdam (`plans`), whose credentials reach the server only as references to the bucket's variables (nobody copied a key). The server signs its S3 requests itself (Signature V4 with Node's crypto, no SDK). Each sign-up gets a private link (`/interested/#mine=…`): the secret is after the #, so it never reaches a server log, and the page sends it in a header; with it people see or withdraw their sign-up, which deletes the plans at once. The admin page (`brieltje.be/admin`, the game's admin login) shows the numbers (sign-ups, people in all, who wants their house in, which days), every sign-up with search and a filter, a status (new, contacted, modelled, declined) and a private note, the plans as five-minute download links, and delete. Guarded against: cross-site posts (same origin only), bots (a trap field), floods (5 sign-ups an hour per address, 300 a day in all, 30 uploads an hour, 3 at once, 4 GB in all), files that aren't plans (the type comes from the first bytes, never the name; stored under random names; always downloaded as attachments, so nothing uploaded can run as a page), guessing someone's link (144 random bits, kept only as a hash, compared in constant time), leaking the bucket's keys (errors log only S3's error code), and a race (found by the automated security review) where an upload still running when someone withdrew could leave their plan behind in the bucket: one upload at a time per sign-up, and a file is recorded in one database statement only while the sign-up exists and has room, else it is deleted from the bucket at once. Tested in production against the bucket: a test sign-up, a PDF stored, seen through its link, withdrawn and deleted. The privacy policy has a section for it. A local test passes 26 checks. |

The traffic now stops for people and for cars someone drives, never for a parked one. The Sound button shows only while a menu is open (Shift+M while playing); on a phone Full screen moves up next to ☰. The big map opens by the letter M on the key rather than its place, because AZERTY keyboards (most of Belgium) have M where QWERTY has the semicolon; movement stays by place, so ZQSD on AZERTY is WASD. |
| 2026-10-31 | **The admin page shows how much the game is played, the login log and who is online; the privacy policy covers everything the GDPR asks** | Yoshi wants the audit log, the logins and basic numbers on the popularity of the game on brieltje.be/admin. Four tabs: Overview, Sign-ups, Logins, Online now. The numbers are counted without cookies and without keeping anything about a person (`web/server/stats.mjs`): page loads and visitors of the game and the interest page (a visitor is a hash of the address and the browser's name with a salt that changes every day and is never stored, so visitors can't be traced or matched across days), games started and minutes played (the page reports them, with only what the player chose: mode, controls, alone or together, language; nothing read from the device), and the most players at once. Only daily totals are stored. Bots and browsers that send Global Privacy Control or Do Not Track aren't counted. A flood of reports can't inflate the numbers much: 20 starts and 70 minutes an hour per address, and only known values count, so nothing typed is ever stored. The overview shows eight numbers, four bar charts of the last 30 days (each also as a table) and how people play. The privacy policy was rewritten for articles 13 and 14: the legal basis per use, the legitimate interests, the recipients and the transfers outside the EU (Railway's data processing agreement uses the EU standard contractual clauses or the Data Privacy Framework; checked on railway.com), whether you have to give anything, no automated decisions, every right and how to use it, the one-month answer, the Data Protection Authority's address, children, and changes. Withdrawing a sign-up now deletes its row first and its plans after; a plan whose delete fails is kept on a list in the database and tried again until it's gone. The own sign-up page shows labelled rows, and the start screen links to the sign-up page. |
| 2026-10-31 | **The game in English and Dutch** | Yoshi asked for a fully Dutch game, the language picked on the start screen and in the settings. `web/src/i18n.ts` holds both dictionaries, 482 texts each, and `t(key, params)`; the Dutch one must have every English key with the same {placeholders}, or `tsc` fails and names the key. The static HTML carries `data-i18n` attributes. A switch fills them again and every module redraws its own text (`onLang`), so nothing reloads and multiplayer keeps its connection. The choice is kept as `briel.lang` (`en` or `nl`), the key the legal pages read, and their links get `?lang=`; a first visit follows the browser (Dutch for nl, nl-BE). A tiny `public/lang-early.js` puts the loading screen and the tab's title in Dutch before the game's script arrives (a file, not inline: the CSP allows no inline script). Messages from the server keep their English text and add a `code` with parameters (names, numbers, a team, a game, lists of names); the page words them in its own language and shows the English text for a code it doesn't know, so old and new pages and servers mix safely during a deploy. Names from the data are translated in maps by their English name or id, with the data's own name as the fallback: floors and surfaces (the rooms are Dutch), doors, cars, and the planner's furniture by item id, so `shared/furniture.json` stays as it is and a new piece shows its English name until it gets a Dutch one (the dev build warns). The Dutch is Flemish usage: gsm, zetel, enkel, eender welk, trager, errond, notelaar, km/u, pincode, beheerder for the admin, verstoppertje with "Wie niet weg is, is gezien!", a decimal comma. Dutch prompts put a colon after the key ("Gebruik: uitstappen"), and the three longest words on the small round touch buttons are a size smaller. Security (XSS): translated text only ever goes into the page as text; the big map's title and legend, built with `innerHTML` before, are elements now. The server's parameters are checked on arrival (short strings, finite numbers, true or false, short lists of names), else the English text shows. No new message goes from the page to the server. The key hints follow the keyboard (`web/src/keys.ts`): Chrome's layout map where it has one, else a setting (Automatic, AZERTY, QWERTY, kept as `briel.keyboard`), Automatic meaning AZERTY for French and Belgian Dutch browsers; only the labels change, as the game reads keys by their place. The phone overlap check stays at 0 in both languages (7 sizes, 15 states), and a new check for text wider than its box found only those three buttons. |

| 2026-10-31 | **The houses round the two portals at home: our own Blender models** | Yoshi asked for more detail round the portals, and for the houses there to look really nice and realistic. Walking round both portals showed the neighbours as the open data built them: walls pushed up from the GRB outlines, flat dark windows with a sill stuck on, one generated brick, roofs without ridge tiles, verges or fascias. The corner house across the lane was even the wrong shape: the laser scan of 2013-2015 is older than it. Street View (April 2023, through the extension), the orthophoto and the GRB gave what is really there; houses are named by where they stand, never by their numbers. `tools/homestreet/houses.py` builds them in Blender. The corner house across the lane: two storeys of brown-grey wire-cut brick on a dark plinth, its gable to the lane rendered cream with weathered vertical boards at the top, the front door and three windows upstairs on the side road, a garage wing behind, solar panels, roof windows and a steel flue. Its link along the lane is cream render under a flat roof, with the heat pump's outdoor unit on it. Beside the house portal stands its old barn of red-brown brick with solar panels on both slopes. Over the side road, seen from the portal, is a house of one and a half storeys in yellow-beige brick with roof windows, a cross-gabled wing and a long lean-to shed. Past the junction stand two new-builds in pale grey brick with hip roofs. The bungalow's neighbour is red brick under a hip roof of orange-red pantiles, with its chimney, the round-headed door in a white stone surround, two wall lanterns, light grey frames, a conservatory, and in front a steel bar fence on a low plinth, brick pillars with bluestone caps, the gate and the letterbox in the pillar. The bungalow itself keeps its walls and gets ridge tiles, white bargeboards and fascias, a cap and two pots on its chimney, and a terrace of light slabs edged with clipped box in front of its three tall windows, where the ragged yard paving of the old house lay. By the house portal there is a low rendered garden wall along the lane, as Street View shows it, and a stack of logs against the barn. Every opening has a reveal, a frame with its sashes, glass set back and a bluestone sill (doors a step); every roof has tiles with a roll, ridge tiles, verges, a fascia, a soffit, gutters and downpipes. The brick (red-orange multi-tone, and a light wire-cut one tinted per house), roof tile and timber textures are our own (`tools/homestreet/textures.py`); render and paving use the game's. Ambient occlusion is baked in Cycles into a colour per corner, so reveals, eaves and wall feet are shaded at no cost in the game; the walls are cut into 60 cm cells for it. Each of the two clusters is one mesh per material (12 meshes, about 35,000 triangles); beyond 150 m its trim (frames, gutters, fences) is hidden, and beyond the draw distance the whole cluster. `web/src/world/homeStreet.ts` stops drawing the open-data blocks of these buildings, which stay for walking into; the pillars, fences, garden wall and logs have blocks of their own. If the model doesn't load, the open-data houses are drawn as before. Nothing stands on a road, a portal's pad or where travellers arrive, and the parked Tesla and Model 3 are untouched. The models join the ground's baked shading and are targets for shots. Cost, measured in the headless browser by swapping models and open-data houses within one page (1280 x 720, blocks of 120 frames in ABBA order, the lowest tenth of each; other sessions loaded the graphics chip, so the calmest blocks count): Low at the house portal 10.8 ms before and 10.4 to 11.0 after, the start view 11.8 and 11.7, behind the portal 9.5 and 9.7, the bungalow portal 9.2 and 9.0; Medium 16.7 and 16.8, 17.0 and 17.1, 14.4 and 14.8, 12.6 and 12.1. Draw calls (all passes) change by -3 to +3, triangles by +36,000 at the house and +15,000 at the bungalow. Download after the start click: 0.75 MB more on a computer (the model 0.35 MB compressed, the textures 0.40 MB), 0.48 MB on a phone (half-size textures). The start screen's bungalow slide shows the bungalow's new terrace and roof trim and its neighbour, so it needs a new render. |
| 2026-10-31 | **Molenhoek round its portal: its houses and its rebuilt street, our own Blender model** | Yoshi asked for more detail round the portals, with houses that look real, made as our own Blender models. Round the Molenhoek portal a survey of Street View (August 2026, through the Claude extension: the headless browser gets no images) showed that the street itself has changed: it was rebuilt this year (the GRB's centre line there has a version of February 2026). It now has a dark asphalt carriageway with a concrete gutter and drains, a raised red cycle lane on either side, a kerb and a pavement of grey concrete pavers, slim steel lamp posts and no overhead cables. Where the new stretch ends towards the church (x 690) a traffic island with a keep-left sign marks the old concrete road. Street View has no 2026 images east of about x 790; looking east from there the new layout carries on as far as can be seen, so the model runs it to the end of the area (x 886), which is an assumption. `tools/molenhoek_survey.py` writes `data/molenhoek.json`: the centre line and the free width to the plots on either side (GRB Wegsegment and ADP), and per house its plan from the village's blocks (the GRB outline, the laser scan's heights, the front wall found from the street), which walls stand against a neighbour, and what the survey saw. Houses are keyed by position and named by where they stand (`sw_tall`, `n_pair_east`), never by number; no images or addresses are kept. `tools/molenhoek.py` builds the seventeen houses nearest the portal on those plans: the tall red house west of the portal with its white stone bands, oriel and flat cornice; the pairs either side with their bricks, doors and solar panels; the house in yellow brick with cream boards above and the red one with the steep gable east of it; the white cube with its carport; the two bungalows with their white fascias; the house with the slate roof, dormer, black shutters and a front gable with a pink tip; the one with orange pantiles and round-headed windows; the 1970s house with its dark bands and porch. Each has recessed windows with frames, mullions, sills, roller shutters and their boxes, net curtains (lighter panes), doors with steps, plinths, lintels, gutters, downpipes, ridge caps, chimneys, roof windows; plainer windows on the sides and backs; the lower parts behind under flat roofs. It also builds the street from the island to x 886 with its drains, the island, a fire hydrant, an electricity cabinet, a warning sign, and eight conifers along the empty plot where the portal stands and in the front gardens, where the laser scan of 2013-2015 still saw broad trees (those five give way). In the game (`web/src/world/molenhoek.ts`, loaded with the church and the school) the village's blocks, roofs and windows inside the houses' outlines and the pavings under the new street aren't drawn but stay for walking into, the nine concrete poles along the stretch become steel lamp posts and lose their cables, the old road is cut along the relief's 4 m squares (it was 8 m) so it stays under the new one, and the garden walls, posts, cabinet and the new surfaces' heights are there for walking and driving. Each textured mesh names a shared game texture that its vertex colours tint, so every brick and roof colour is one draw call: ten meshes, 21,600 triangles, the small things (frames, gutters, railings, signs) only within 200 m. The one new texture is a generated asphalt (`tools/textures.py`), tinted grey for the road and red for the cycle lanes. Downloads, fetched with the village after play has begun: the model 1.67 MB (287 KB sent compressed), the asphalt 435 KB (157 KB on phones). Cost, measured with the graphics chip's own timer in the headless browser (1280 x 860; Low draws 704 px wide, Medium 1024), each page started in its preset, the game's own dip of the resolution reset before each measurement, the lowest tenth of 240 frames, before and after in alternating page loads, two rounds. Low at the portal's arrival view: 6.49 and 6.95 ms before, 6.65 and 6.80 after; looking down the street from its east end: 6.83 and 6.68 before, 6.78 and 6.42 after. Medium at the portal: 11.47 and 11.31 before, 11.47 and 11.83 after; down the street: 10.71 and 10.92 before, 10.17 and 10.92 after. That is at most +0.3 ms on average, within the 0.3 to 0.5 ms that runs of the same page differ by. Showing and hiding the model within one page gives +0.7 ms (Low) and +1.4 ms (Medium) at the portal and -0.2 and +0.3 ms down the street, but that sets the houses, conifers and street against bare ground and sky, as their blocks, trees and the old road stay hidden. At the portal it adds 1 to 7 draw calls and about 7,000 triangles (of 820,000); down the street up to 12 draw calls and 34,000 triangles. Switching from Low to Medium in a running page drew only the sky in this headless browser, with or without this model; that wasn't looked into. The portal's spot and its arrival view stay clear; nothing new stands on the carriageway, so the traffic drives as before. |

The looks come from Street View (August 2026 where the car passed, April 2023 or older elsewhere), read per house: brick colour or paint, plinths, the openings per storey, frames, sills, brick lintels, shutters, painted surrounds, doors with fanlights, garage doors, shop windows, carport bays, chimneys, roof windows, gutters and downpipes, wall anchors, the flats' glass railing. The script packs the GLB itself: positions as 16-bit integers (KHR_mesh_quantization), no normals (everything is flat and drawn with flat shading), colours as bytes. The game (`web/src/world/townCentre.ts`, a chunk of 1.6 KB) draws it with five draw calls near (brick, render, tiles, trim, glass) and a plain copy of the same volumes with three beyond 230 m from the square. The open-data blocks inside the houses' outlines are left out of the drawing and stay for walking and driving into. New on the square: three bollards along the long house, two planters and a bench before the corner shop, the café's flags and planter, each a block to walk into, at least 3 m from the traffic's lanes and 19 m from the portals' arrival spots. The square's grey pavers are darker, as in the photos, and the church's slates darker and duller. A bug turned up on the way: when Auto dropped a graphics preset away from the house, the sun's shadow map was thrown away and never made again (its update is off there), and everything that takes the shadow drew nothing, so the screen emptied in the village; the map is now rebuilt at once. Draw calls: 5 more near the square; 43,000 triangles (3 to 7 % of a frame's). The admin's new house rule "Child-friendly for everyone" (`paintball` in `RULES`, off by default) makes every page show Paintball and greys out Action; players keep their own pick for when it is off again. Cost: the splats are one instanced draw call whose fade runs on the graphics card (120 at most, the oldest replaced), the flying balls another (48 at most), blobs are capped at 40 small meshes, and nothing adds a light or a full-screen pass. All sounds are made in code, so there are no new assets. The terms say Paintball is for everyone; Action stays advised from 12.
