Round the portal: the white half of the pair west of it has its entry recessed by the pair's middle beside its big window, and the brick wall with piers stands in front of it; the taupe half has pale stone lintels, top lights and a hedge instead of that wall; the light yellow 1960s half has two windows behind closed grey roller shutters and a glazed door, not one glass front. Opposite, the 1970s house has an open carport under its upper floor with a pier at the corner, its green door and a slit window; the house done up in black has its black garage door in the low link with a window over it; the darker half of the red pair has its box hedge and a box ball. East of the portal: the low wall in front of the cream-boarded house has its piers; the narrow brown house has its door at the east end, its chimney at the west and low golden shrubs; the house with the gable dormer and the one with the white cornice stood on each other's plots and are swapped, and the dormer house has its french balcony; the pair under slate has a french balcony and shrubs; across the street the front-gabled house and the orange row have box hedges, the grey-brown house three windows upstairs, the red half of the hipped pair its openings framed in orange-red brick, a tall hedge and a conifer, and the plainer house near the junction its door on the west and brown frames. Towards the church square: the white gable house has its raised ground floor up four steps, three windows upstairs and one high in the gable, and a planter wall with a hedge; the taupe house a white post-and-rail fence; the long low house and shop a cross gable with its window, its shuttered window, the vending machine where it stands and two wide windows at its east end; the large old house three bays with the door in the middle, segmental stone arches over every opening, and cream double garage doors in its lower part; the brown house under pantiles soldier-course lintels. By the junction: the pair with the bay has glazed double doors, shutters, a hydrangea and a box hedge round the lawn; the two pairs with steep outer gables have french windows under the gables (the corner pair's behind closed white shutters), their doors the right colours, browner brick on the corner, two columnar conifers and box hedges. The builder learnt these as options any house can use (top lights, french balconies, shutter colours, stone arches and lintels, steps to a raised door, hedges, box balls, garden conifers, fences, the wall and street-side openings of the lower parts); the hedges keep behind the plot line, so the pavements, the cycle lanes and the portal's arrival views stay clear, and each brings a block for walking into. Only shapes and colours were taken from the photos: no Street View imagery, no numbers, no lettering. The model is now 54,270 triangles in twelve meshes (2,371 more), 4.21 MB (196 KB more; 1,041 KB gzipped, 52 KB more), no new textures. Cost, measured as before (the graphics chip's timer, each page started in its preset, the lowest tenth of 240 frames), the new model against the old one in alternating page loads. Low at the portal's arrival view: 5.35, 5.55 and 5.29 ms new, 5.47, 5.47 and 5.33 old; down the street from x 842: 5.41, 6.12 and 5.36 new, 5.50, 5.42 and 5.40 old. Medium at the portal: 10.29 and 10.36 new, 10.22 and 10.95 old; down the street: 9.81 and 9.90 new, 9.89 and 10.40 old. Within the run-to-run noise everywhere (the one high Low reading came with a slow round for the whole page), well under the 1 ms allowed. |
| 2026-10-31 | **Multiplayer at 20 updates a second, the others drawn a tenth of a second behind, in about half the bytes** | Yoshi asked what could make multiplayer faster and more responsive, ahead of a meetup with up to 16 players on one server. Before, each page sent its position 10 times a second, and 10 times a second the server sent every page everyone's full state as plain JSON, with all four cars, the three horses and every strongman, parked or not. A page slid each figure from where it was to the newest position over 0.1 s, so the others were drawn 140 ms behind on a quiet line and stopped and lurched whenever a packet came early or late. Now each page sends 20 times a second while anything changes, once more when it stops, then about three times a second, each state with the page's own time for it. The server lines that clock up with its own (by the quickest message seen), so a late packet keeps its true moment; the stamp is always between half a second ago and now and is only used for drawing, never to check anything (the movement budget, hits and trips work per second as before). Twenty times a second the server sends one tick of compact arrays (`web/shared/wire.mjs`): a player when they sent something new or changed, a car or horse when its driver or rider did, the strongmen every other tick when they moved, everything once a second in turn and everything to a page that just connected, never a page's own player; positions to the centimetre, angles to the milliradian. The page draws everything a little in the past on the server's clock (`web/src/net/track.ts`), between two states. The delay follows what the ticks need (one tick, how late they come, how old the states in them are, as 9 ticks in 10 have it): about 100 to 130 ms on a steady line. A late packet is bridged by carrying on at the last speed for at most 120 ms, and the difference is taken out over 0.1 s; a respawn or a portal trip jumps, by a teleport counter in the state. Cars someone else drives (also seen from the passenger seat), ridden horses and the strongmen use the same tracks. The server pings each page every 2 seconds (a random number, timed on the server, counted once), shown in the FPS line (F), in `?diag` (with the delay and the bytes a second) and per player on the admin page. Pages from before this get the old full states 10 times a second until they reload, so a deploy breaks nobody; a page that finds another protocol in the welcome asks for a reload. The flood guard counts states apart (30 a second) from everything else (40, as before). No compression on the socket, as before: chat shares it with the game state (BREACH). Measured with script players on a local server, bytes the server sends each page, before and after: 2 players 12.5 and 3.1 KB/s, 8 players 21.5 and 11.4, 16 all walking 36.7 and 19.0, 16 with half of them standing 35.4 and 13.8, with twice the updates. Smoothness, a page drawing a script player who walks a known circle at 4 m/s (timers accurate to the millisecond, as on Railway's Linux): on a local line drawn 140 and 97 ms behind, the spread of that 7 and 0.7 ms, frames where the figure stood still 24 of 1130 and none; at 100 ms each way with 5 ms jitter, 160 and 105 ms on top of the network, 23 stalled frames and none; at 100 ms with 30 ms jitter, 178 and 156 ms, the spread 26.5 and 3.5 ms, 139 stalled frames of 1110 and none, the speed from frame to frame 4.0 ± 2.4 and 4.0 ± 0.16 m/s. The Tesla someone else drives at 12 m/s, frame to frame: 12 ± 2.3 and 12 ± 0.6 m/s on a local line (23 stalled frames and none, drawn 148 and 91 ms behind), 12 ± 3.8 and 12 ± 0.5 m/s with 30 ms jitter (55 stalled frames and none); a ridden horse at 7 m/s 7 ± 2.0 and 7 ± 0.35 m/s, a roaming strongman 1.3 ± 0.32 and 1.3 ± 0.12 m/s. The server's time per tick with 16 players and the five strongmen went from 1.0 to 0.6 ms on this PC, twice as often, about 1% of a core either way. Right after joining, while the page loads, the others can be drawn up to 0.4 s behind for a few seconds. |
| 2026-10-31 | **Start screen, menu and HUD checked for the meetup: fixes for phones, joining and the minimap** | Yoshi asked for a check of the start screen, the settings and the other UI for stability, grouping and ease of use, with first-timers of every age in mind. Each part was tried with real clicks, taps and keys in headless Chromium at 1280×800, 1180×820, 844×390 and 390×844, and multiplayer against a local server that was stopped mid-game. **Phone held sideways:** the start screen now fits without scrolling (it was 48 px too tall, so Touch screen was cut off). Your name stands beside Alone / Together and the PIN beside the 13+ tick box. The touch note's small short-screen style had been overruled by a rule further down. The note gives way to the PIN once Together is picked. With Safari's bars showing, the word "Mode" and the sign-up link make way too. Upright, "Start at" no longer runs out of the panel and the title is no longer behind the language buttons. **Joining:** with Together picked, a start button joins first and starts only if that worked; before, typing the PIN and tapping Touch screen started you alone. A second click waits for the join already on its way, so a double click sends one. A missing name or PIN puts the cursor in that field. Enter in the name field starts with the first start button (Touch screen on a phone, not keyboard and mouse). Play alone (was Leave / Stoppen) works at once, also with the server away. **Touch screens:** buttons, fields and tick boxes are 40 to 44 px high; the small links keep their look but get an invisible margin to tap. The slideshow's buttons are bigger and sit in the corner, clear of the panel (they covered its bottom corner on an iPad). **Menu:** Settings has the field of view and the lighting test (Advanced) with the graphics, the language at the top right (on a phone it was below the fold), then mouse and controller together. Keyboard + mouse / controller instead moved from Play to the Controls tab, where you look for it. **HUD:** the minimap names the floor indoors only (it said Ground floor on the church square), its words have a dark edge so they read on the light village, and your arrow has one too (it vanished on the white roads and square). The controller's name bottom left shows for 4 seconds like the other hints; "no controller found" stays while it is missing. **Stability:** the admin's tab and its login log no longer leave unhandled promise rejections when the server is out of reach. Opening and closing the menu many times with Esc, M and the minimap, switching tabs, modes and languages fast, resizing and losing the mouse capture gave no errors. **Wording:** the touch note says "hold your device sideways" (it said phone on an iPad too); Dutch says beheerder, not admin; FPS-teller is spelt one way. Left as advice: the menu can't be worked with a controller beyond the tabs, graphics and walking speed (✕ closes it); the connection line says "Verbinden…" most of the time while the server is away. |

To Kapelleweg: a cottage with a half-timbered gable behind its hedge, a white house with two dormers and brown shutters, a row on the pavement with a pointed gable, a 1930s house with stone bands behind a low wall with piers, the new grey terrace, a long cream farmhouse under pantiles with three gabled dormers and green shutters, and a c. 1900 house with stone arches and keystones over its windows. To Briel and past it: a roof covered in solar panels behind a tall hedge, an old cottage of yellow-brown hand-made brick under pantiles with an arched door, a small house with two white dormers, a house with a crow-stepped gable, a modern white villa, the orange-red house and the old building with a round window by the junction, and the set-back white houses beyond. Four houses are blurred in Street View at their owners' request; two by the meadow near the square are newer than the newest images (the village data has them as a flat-roofed building); a few are hidden behind conifers or stand far back: those keep the village's blocks. The street stays the village's (it was not rebuilt, unlike Molenhoek's); the one thing added there is the give-way sign at the corner where Kapelleweg meets Kapellenhoek, where it stands. Hedges stay behind the plot line and every new solid stands at least 4.5 m from the centre line, so the road and the paths stay walkable; none of these houses is one the town's or Molenhoek's model already has. How it is built: `tools/molenhoek.py` builds either street now, from `data/<place>.json` (`tools/kapellenhoek_survey.py` writes Kapellenhoek's; the per-house plan moved into `plan()` in `tools/molenhoek_survey.py`, which Molenhoek's survey uses unchanged: its data and its model's geometry come out the same). New options: dormers under a gable of their own, half-timbering, a crow-stepped gable, arched doors in a pattern, a give-way sign, fronts counted from their left end as seen from the street, and `lean` (no windows on walls that face away from the street, sills and steps among the small things): Kapellenhoek has 39,400 triangles in ten meshes where it first had 54,500. Both GLBs are packed as the town's is (16-bit positions with KHR_mesh_quantization, byte normals and colours): Kapellenhoek's model is 1.77 MB, sent as 381 KB (brotli; 615 KB gzipped); Molenhoek from 4.21 MB to 2.59 MB, sent as 600 KB instead of 688 KB, so the village's download grows by 293 KB. In the game `web/src/world/molenhoek.ts` became `streetModel.ts`, which loads and fits any such street (the village's blocks, roofs and windows inside the outlines are not drawn but stay for walking into; the detail meshes' distance is measured through the packed mesh's own scale and offset), and `main.ts` loads both streets. Cost, measured as before (the graphics chip's timer, each page in its preset, the lowest tenth of 240 frames), the page with the model and without it (?nokapellenhoek) in alternating loads: Low looking up the street from the square end, 7.19 and 6.88 ms with it, 7.17 and 7.76 without; from Kapelleweg towards Briel, 6.21 and 5.82 with, 5.69 and 6.33 without. Medium, four rounds: from the square end 11.89, 11.61, 9.89 and 10.52 with, 11.98, 10.30, 10.13 and 10.99 without (+0.1 ms); towards Briel 12.56, 12.59, 11.00 and 11.56 with, 12.11, 10.92, 10.84 and 11.03 without (+0.7 ms on average, runs of the same page differing by up to 1.7 ms). Within the 1 ms allowed. |
| 2026-10-31 | **The streets in detail: concrete slabs, asphalt, setts, kerbs, pavements, verges and footpaths, measured on the GRB and made up from a Street View tour** | Yoshi asked for more realistic roads and pavements, from Street View and our own renders, everywhere but Molenhoek's street (another agent rebuilds it). The tour (the newest images, through the Claude extension, for reference only: 2023 on Kapellenhoek and Kapelleweg, 2026 round the school, 2009 on most of Briel) showed that Kapellenhoek is not asphalt but pale concrete slabs, a sealed joint down the middle and across every five metres, cracks sealed in black, a gutter of grey setts with cast-iron gullies, a raised kerb and a narrow pavement of grey pavers where houses stand, a dashed centre line by the church square; Kapelleweg a narrow concrete lane; Briel narrow concrete, widened with asphalt along its edges, the lane by the house old cracked concrete with grass up to it; Kerkhofstraat dark asphalt between light setts; at the school's gate a plateau of red-brown pavers with a zebra crossing; Smetledekouterwegel a path of concrete tiles, Pastorijwegel pavers, Kosterstraatje and Processewijkweg gravel. **Data-driven:** `tools/road_detail.py` cuts every street's centre line (Wegsegment) into stations 2.5 m apart and finds, either side, the carriageway's edge on the GRB's dividing lines (WGO: the carriageway's edge, the pavement's edge where the kerb stands, an unpaved verge) or the drawn road surface's own edge, the pavement's back edge, and drives beyond it (lowered kerbs); streets give way to each other in a fixed order where they meet, kerbs stop at a joining street's mouth, and whatever road surface is left (junctions, widenings, forecourts) is drawn as it is. Each street's look is a profile in `web/src/world/roadDetail.ts`; a street not named gets asphalt with kerbs where the GRB has a pavement, so the map's extension east is covered once the tool runs on its data. **Drawn** as strips across each street with the texture running along it and the wear in the vertex colours (darker wheel tracks and edges, each slab its own shade, patchy concrete), joints, cracks, paint, gullies and manholes as flat shapes a few millimetres up in one matte material, pulled forward in depth and in the ground's shading. The surfaces use the village's own materials (pavers, concrete, gravel, tinted per vertex) and replace the old road blocks they cover (by key; those stay for walking on), so they cost about what the plain road did: on the graphics chip timer at 1600 x 900, in three alternating page loads with and without them (dev `?noroads`), three busy street views on Kapellenhoek and Briel differed by -1.6 to +0.7 ms per round and -0.1 to -0.6 ms on average (Low 6.0 to 8.5 ms, Medium 10.4 to 12.9 ms; noise, if anything cheaper: the road's normal map is half the size), with 6 to 24 more draw calls and about 70,000 more triangles. The pavements and the school's plateau get walking blocks of 6 to 11 cm (the player steps up 50 cm, a car rolls over 30); a car driven down Kapellenhoek's lane hits nothing. **Own textures:** only the concrete road (`road_concrete`, generated in `tools/textures.py`: 307 KB, 54 KB for phones); the data is 41 KB compressed. Molenhoek's street and junction keep the village's road surface (and the GRB's two plateaus there are not drawn). Not done: painted zone-30 markings (the tour saw none at the school, and the game paints no lettering), bollards (nothing new in the way). |

| 2026-11-01 | **Alone, the house and the bungalow are shut: inside only when playing together** | Yoshi asked for the main house and the bungalow to be open in multiplayer only. Playing alone, no door opens. The outside doors (the ones with an outer face) shut at once. No pane breaks, from a bullet, a grenade or a blast. A bullet sparks off the glass instead. Trying a door or shooting a pane shows a two-line message with a red edge: only accessible in multiplayer, and Yoshi's address for anyone who wants to look around solo (Yoshi's wording and address). Dying alone brings you back in the street, the garden or on the side path. Leaving the others while inside puts you out in front of the house you were in, and the house shuts again. Joining opens the doors and the panes as the server has them. A test in the browser walked and flew at every floor towards both buildings from 36 directions, and through each outside door: nobody got in. The rule lives in the page, like everything else in solo play. The insides are not in the page at all (see the rows of 6 November). |
| 2026-11-01 | **Cheap looks: sky in the village's windows with curtains behind them, a warmer grade, drifting clouds, chimney smoke and rippling ponds** | Yoshi asked for cheap improvements that make the game look a lot better, and approved four. The open-data houses' windows were dark holes. Their glass now mirrors about three times more sky, as double glazing does. Each pane leans and bows a little, so neighbouring windows show different sky. About two in three have something behind them: nets (full or the lower half), curtains, venetian or roller blinds, in colours that mostly agree per house and floor. The coverings are eight cells of a small picture painted at load time, so there is no file to fetch. Each pane is now one quad instead of a box, so the glass takes less memory than before (`world/windows.ts`). The colour grade (a touch warmer, more colour where there is little, a faint vignette) sits inside the output pass, so it adds no pass (`src/grade.ts`). The clouds drift about 5 degrees back and forth over five minutes. The sun, the sky round it and the horizon stay put, so the sun and the shadows still agree. Cloud shadows on the ground were the other option. They were left out because they would need every ground and wall material changed, and the ground's are being reworked separately. The sky is now drawn by our own shader after everything solid, in place of three.js's background (`world/sky.ts`). It shades only the pixels nothing covers, and the 100 MB cube of 32-bit floats it was turned into is gone. That saving pays for the rest. Smoke rises from two chimneys on the church square, on Medium and up, not on phones, within 250 m. The ponds ripple in the wind: only their normals move, and the ripples fade where they get too small to draw (`world/water.ts`). Cost, measured with the graphics chip's timer in one page with the looks on and off in shuffled blocks (1600 by 900), in Kapellenhoek, on the church square and from the air. All four together save time: 0.33 to 0.59 ms on Low (medians) and 0.67 to 0.80 ms on Medium (the fastest fifth of frames, as other work shared the graphics chip). The grade costs 0.04 to 0.16 ms. The windows, water and smoke stay within the noise. The sky saves 0.25 to 0.9 ms. Download +3.6 KB brotli, no new files. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Trees with leaves: clumps of leaves on every crown, a ragged outline, each tree its own; the wind on Low too** | Yoshi named the ball-shaped crowns the biggest "this is a game" giveaway and asked for real-looking trees, cheaply, with a gentle wind in them and in the hedges. **Shapes** (`surroundings.ts`): a crown is now balls of leaves on a shell round a core, the core drawn last so the graphics chip skips what the balls hide of it. Near the house and the church it is a core and 12 to 14 balls of 80 triangles; elsewhere a core of 80 and five of 20; a tall tree's lower boughs are three of 20. The shapes are indexed, so every corner is worked out once instead of once per triangle: 102 corners for a simple crown instead of 240, 546 for a full one instead of 1440. Their normals lie halfway between each ball's and the whole crown's, so a crown is lit as one crown, not as a heap of balls. They are darker deep inside and underneath, and each ball has a shade of its own. The old random shade per corner, which turned every triangle into a facet, is gone. Each crown is a little wider one way than the other, taller or flatter, and leans a few degrees, all from its place, so three shapes read as many; each green tree also has its own shade, lighter or darker, yellower or bluer (`wind.ts`, beside the autumn colours). The crowns reach as wide as the old lumps did. Trunks darken towards the crown. The hedges' leafy lumps, and with them the garden's shrubs, are smooth balls with leaves instead of flat-faced ones. **Leaves** (`world/leaves.ts`): a 256 px tile of leaf clumps drawn in code at the start (about 18 ms; nothing to download), laid on from three sides in metres, so no ball has a seam or a pinch. The clumps are about 40 cm on trees and 14 cm on hedge lumps. Where a crown turns away from the eye only the clumps' middles are drawn, so its outline comes out ragged, clump by clump. It is an opaque cut; nothing is blended. **What it costs:** a cut stops the graphics chip from skipping a crown's hidden pixels early, which alone cost 0.4 ms in the thick of the woods on Medium. The leaves pay for it by taking the light as plain diffuse surfaces: at roughness 0.95 their gloss was a few percent of their light, and dropping its sums and look-ups saved about 0.8 ms there. Timed in one page with the graphics chip's timer, the whole change against the old trees, trunks, hedge lumps and hedges swapped in turn (and the wind off for the old on Low), 16 bursts of 20 frames, the median of each burst's 10th percentile, new minus old in ms, Low then Medium: the woods from their edge -0.4 and -0.45; under the crowns -0.3 and -0.3; the street at the house -0.5 and -0.35; the trees near the house -0.4 and -0.4; Kapellenhoek -0.1 and -0.05; the church square -0.15 and -0.15; the village from the air -0.1 to -0.4 and -0.15; the woods from the air -0.15 and +0.08 (wider crowns seen from above). **Wind:** on Low too now, since it costs under 0.1 ms there (it is still off on phones), so Low, which Auto picks on weak graphics chips, shows the crowns swaying and the hedge lumps trembling too. The plain hedge blocks (kilometres of them, without lumps) read their textures a few millimetres off, in a ripple that follows the gusts: their leaves tremble while nothing moves, so no gap can open along their edges. **Bytes:** nothing more to download; the code grows by 1.6 KB (brotli). |
| 2026-11-01 | **Contact shadows on the ground; the fields' crops from the farmers' declarations; lawns in stripes, meadows and rough margins** | Yoshi approved two cheap upgrades: a soft dark band where houses, walls, hedges, fences and sheds meet the ground (they stood on a hard line), and fields and lawns that were large flat surfaces of one colour. **Contact shadows**, baked into the ground's shading (`web/src/world/groundShade.ts`): the static scene is drawn from above once more without the repeated shapes (tree crowns and trunks, the hedges' leafy lumps, posts), and each spot of the ground looks in 16 directions out to 2 m for how near and how tall what stands there is. The band reaches 80 cm from a low fence and 2 m from a house wall. It dims the sky light by up to four fifths and the sunlight by up to half: real sunlight isn't dimmed there, but the eye reads the band as weight, and on light paving in the sun a weaker band didn't show. The texture already read per pixel carries it, so it costs nothing while playing; the garden's own drive, paths and borders read it now too. **Crops:** `tools/crops.py` fetches the Landbouwgebruikspercelen (Departement Landbouw en Visserij, open data under the Modellicentie Gratis Hergebruik; only the crop's name is kept, nothing about who farms it) for the map's area: 379 parcels. Lede is nursery country: avenue trees, tagetes (the nurseries' nematode crop), hedge plants and shrubs cover most fields, then maize, beet and chicory, fruit trees, roses, cover crops, two winter cereals and a field of leeks; 145 parcels are grassland. `tools/street.py` splits each field by the parcels on it, each crop a field of its own half a metre in from where they meet, keeps the photo's surface where nothing is declared, and leaves declared grassland as grass. Each crop looks as it does at the end of October (`web/src/world/fields.ts`): maize stubble, winter cereal just up, beet's broad leaves in rows 48 cm apart, leeks on their ridges, tagetes dense and dark with the last orange and yellow flowers, cover crops, nurseries in rows from 2 m (avenue trees) to 60 cm (hedge plants), green, turning or beech-brown. Plants are matte: the soil's sky reflection washed the green crops out. The rows run along the whole field's long side, worked out in Python, where a field cut into pieces by the tiles used to turn its rows at every cut. The big map shows the nurseries and green crops as crops. **Lawns and meadows:** the lawn texture is now ambientCG's Grass004 (CC0), short green grass, made matte (its own roughness put a white patch of sun on every lawn); Poly Haven's leafy_grass before it was half dead leaves and read as hay. About half the gardens are mown in stripes 50 to 95 cm wide along the plot, the others not (robot mowers, ours among them). Declared grassland and fallow are a little rougher. Rough, yellower grass with tussocks grows along field edges, ditches, ponds and field tracks, and weeds creep a ragged metre into the fields. All of it is drawn into the baked texture's fourth channel, from the data and from the fields, tracks and water drawn from above. Within 80 m the grass reads its texture once more at 3.4 times the size against its repeats (not on phones). **The rest of the map:** the fine areas (240 m round the house, the village, Molenhoek's far stretch) left the fields between them unshaded; a coarse texture of the whole map (a metre a texel, two on a phone) now covers the ground beyond them. **Fixes found on the way:** the sun test's nine taps hatched the fields and pavers in a fine pattern (each tap looked at the ground beside it in the sun's image, higher or lower on a slope); each tap is now a ground spot of its own, so fields and pavers are no longer partly shaded. One flock of sheep grazed what is declared a shrub nursery: `tools/countryside.py` was run again (the herds keep to the meadows; crows now also take bare fields of 1,500 m², since many bare fields turned out to be nurseries). **Cost**, on the graphics chip timer within one page (headless 1600 x 900, the grass detail and the whole-map texture on and off in alternating bursts of 40 frames, 16 rounds each way, the fastest tenth of each burst, medians): fields round the house +0.05 ms on Low and +0.16 ms on Medium; from the air over the fields +0.12 and +0.46 ms (the whole-map texture 0.14 of that, the grass 0.21, in runs of their own); from the air over the village +0.03 and +0.05 ms; the church square and Molenhoek under 0.03 ms either way. Baking an area takes 130 ms instead of 57 on this PC, and the whole map 125 to 150 ms more when the village loads. Graphics memory: 22 MB for the whole-map texture (5.6 MB on phones). Download: the start's code with the house data +12 KB and the village's +9 KB (gzip); the lawn's textures are 152 KB smaller (17 KB on phones). Shared data regenerated: `data/street.json`, `web/src/data/house.json`, `village.json`, `countryside.json`. Not done: standing crops in 3D (no new solids), row directions read from the orthophoto. |

The card asks once per browser, at a quiet moment: not while down, driving, riding along, furnishing, typing or travelling. It doesn't come back after closing or sending, nor after a reload. | 2026-11-01 | **The meetup email is on by default on the interest page (opt-out)** | Yoshi asked for the box "Mail me once the meetup is planned" to be ticked from the start. A pre-ticked box isn't valid consent under the GDPR (Planet49, recital 32), so the email no longer rests on consent. It rests on legitimate interest. It is one email about the meetup the person signs up for, with its date and place. It is not marketing. The sign-up itself already covers being contacted about the meetup. The box stays, so anyone can untick it when signing up. Their own link turns it off later. The server still keeps the time it was switched on. Sign-ups from before keep the choice they made. | 2026-11-01 | **The new terrace at Molenhoek's far end, as it stands: dark brown and light beige brick in large blocks under flat roofs** | Yoshi sent photos taken from the street of the new terraced houses near the junction with Oordegemkouter and asked for them to look like that. Street View has only 2009 images there, so the survey had described the houses that stood on the spot before: a white house with brown shutters behind a garden wall, a red brick house under a pitched roof, and a white cottage under pantiles. The red house had its front on its party wall: Molenhoek's centre line ends in front of the row, and for a house past its end the edge facing the line's last point is a side. The GRB has had all five houses of the row since 2018, of one depth and on one front line. Three were asked for. The other two are the same block, mirrored, and left as they were they would have stood as a red block taller than the row and a pantiled cottage against its flat roof, so they are done too. **Now:** two storeys under flat roofs with a thin dark coping, all on one floor level, in two bricks laid in large blocks. A pair in dark chocolate-brown brick has a band of light beige brick across the middle, a pair the other way round, and the end house beside the neighbour's garden is in the light brick with a dark band from its free corner to its garage. The bands run from the ground floor's lintels to the first floor's sills, between the outer edges of each pair's doors. Soldier courses of the light brick over every opening, pale stone sills, anthracite windows of one light, aluminium doors with three frosted panes and a long bar handle (the end house's with a frosted side light), an anthracite sectional garage door, letterboxes and bell plates. The end wall by the neighbour's garden is blank; the photos don't show it. Measured on the photos: the heights on the end house seen square on (the coping at 6.05 m, door and window heads at 2.16, the band from 2.37 to 3.77, the first floor's windows from 3.84 to 5.08), the widths along the pairs through the perspective of the photos taken at an angle (a projective fit scaled by the GRB's fronts; the party wall it puts between the brown pair falls on the joint seen in the brickwork). Nothing of the photos is kept, and there are no numbers or lettering. **How:** `tools/molenhoek.py` can now lay rectangles of another brick into a wall itself (no layer in front of it, so nothing flickers from afar) and soldier courses over the openings. It also has frosted aluminium doors with a side light, sectional doors in a given number of panels, windows of one light, pale sills, small fixtures on a front, a thinner coping and blank end walls; houses given one `level` share a floor (`tools/molenhoek_survey.py`). The houses are described in `tools/molenhoek_end_survey.py` (`_terrace`), each on its GRB outline with its front given. The other street models build byte for byte as before. The tints are set for the game's light, which puts blue sky on this front in the shade, so the bricks come out in the photos' colours. **Cost:** `molenhoek_end3` keeps its 10 draw calls, with 17,509 triangles instead of 17,622 and 169 KB brotli instead of 170 KB. Not done: in front of the two houses past the end of the centre line the ground is still the village's, a plain pale surface at the junction and pavers without the red cycle lane the photos show (the rebuilt street ends with the centre line). |
| 2026-11-01 | **Brieltje's look: the village at dusk with its lights coming on; one typeface, a wordmark with two lit windows, the game called Brieltje everywhere** | Yoshi asked for a professional, less geeky and more welcoming start screen, menu and settings, a nicer brand, and the name Brieltje all across. **Direction:** the start picture is the hero, so the panels stay calm. They are slate blue like the roofs after sunset (`--dusk` #16202a), with warm off-white text (#f5efe4). The first start button is the one to press: keyboard and mouse, touch on a phone or tablet, or the controller once one is connected. It fills with light as the world loads. The sign-up link moved under the start buttons as a quieter row. Laptops up to 900 px high get a tighter layout, so Together's PIN box fits without scrolling. **Menu:** an opaque panel (no blur over the live view; the GPU is the limit) with the wordmark, a darker rail of tabs, settings as rows with switches and filled sliders (`--fill` from `menu.ts`), and the controls as plain lists. **HUD:** the wordmark top left with a dark outline for bright skies; nothing in it moves, and no filters. **Rename:** the page title, meta, manifest, i18n texts and the brand on the interest, legal and admin pages say Brieltje. The hamlet keeps its name in place lines, portals, "Smetlede en Briel" and the bungalow's location. Storage keys, cookies, `window.briel` and code names are unchanged. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Two more portals along Molenhoek: its middle and its end** | Yoshi asked for portals at the middle and the end of Molenhoek. The street is nearly a kilometre long. "Middle of Molenhoek" stands halfway along it, by length. You come out on the pavement looking down the street towards its end. "End of Molenhoek" stands by the new terrace near Oordegemkouter. You come out on the pavement in front of the end house, looking at it. Both pads stand on the edge of the pavement, never on the road. | 2026-11-01 | **Admin stats to trust: the day's visitor code kept for that Belgian day only, players next to visitors, a period switch, hours, tabs that fit a phone** | Yoshi found "today" too high and the stats hard to find their way in. "Today" already began at Belgian midnight; the page now says so ("Since 00:00, Belgian time"). The real fault: the salt behind a visitor's daily hash lived in memory only, and every redeploy (about 12 on 1 Nov) made a new one, so each returning visitor counted again. Now the store keeps a random seed per Belgian day and that day's hashes (tables `visit_seed` and `visit_seen`; `visits` in the file store), and deletes both within a minute after midnight and at start. The salt is the seed run through a key made from `ADMIN_PASSWORD` (a Railway variable, never in the database), so a copy of the database alone can't rebuild a hash. The first seed stored for a day wins and a hash is added in one statement, so Railway's overlapping servers share the seed and count a visitor once. Totals go to the store every 20 s, in one statement per day (a failed write is kept and tried again whole), and on SIGTERM after the counting still waiting for the store. **Trade-off:** during the day, someone holding both the database and the admin password could brute-force which IPv4 address and browser name visited; after midnight nobody can. The database already holds IP addresses (login log, feedback), so that adds little. A cookie or a browser ID was ruled out: in Belgium it needs prior consent and a banner, and Yoshi wants no banner. **Counting:** "Players" (browsers that started a game, once a day, by the same kind of hash) is the headline, next to "Visitors" (opened the page), so bots and link previews only inflate visitors. Not counted: requests with the admin's session (visits, games and the solo log line), prefetch and prerender, bots, GPC and DNT. Per Belgian hour: visitors, players, games and minutes (`*_hHH` keys in `daily`). "Different players" counts distinct names in the login log over the period (together, and alone as typed), the admin's names left out; only counts leave the server. Several days add up each day's count, labelled "once a day each". **Admin page:** a period switch (today, yesterday, 7 days, 30 days, remembered) that every number follows, each compared with the period before (today with yesterday until the same time, from the hours); ten headline numbers; "When people play" by the hour; 30 days of bars with the period lit and a tooltip per day (a tap on a phone); "How we count" behind an info toggle. The tabs scroll sideways with a fade at the edge, the tab bar sticks, the address's hash picks the tab (#logins), back and forward work, and the Scoreboard link shows on phones. Old days without hours or players show "–" or an empty hour chart with a note. Tested with unit checks on the file store and on Postgres 17 SQL (PGlite), and end to end in headless Chromium at 390, 768 and 1440 px. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Admin stats to trust: the day's visitor code kept for that Belgian day only, players next to visitors, a period switch, hours, tabs that fit a phone** | Yoshi found "today" too high and the stats hard to find their way in. "Today" already began at Belgian midnight; the page now says so ("Since 00:00, Belgian time"). The real fault: the salt behind a visitor's daily hash lived in memory only, and every redeploy (about 12 on 1 Nov) made a new one, so each returning visitor counted again. Now the store keeps a random seed per Belgian day and that day's hashes (tables `visit_seed` and `visit_seen`; `visits` in the file store), and deletes both within a minute after midnight and at start. The salt is the seed run through `STATS_SECRET` (32 or more random bytes, a Railway variable, never in the database), so a copy of the database alone can't rebuild a hash. A first version made that key from `ADMIN_PASSWORD`; a review caught it as an offline password-guessing oracle: with a database copy, anyone knows their own address and browser name, so they could hash their own visit under each guessed password until it matched a stored hash. The key is now its own random secret. Without `STATS_SECRET` (local, or before it is set) nothing is stored: the salt and hashes stay in memory as before, the server logs one warning without the value, and the admin page says restarts can count some visitors twice. The first seed stored for a day wins and a hash is added in one statement, so Railway's overlapping servers share the seed and count a visitor once. Totals go to the store every 20 s, in one statement per day (a failed write is kept and tried again whole), and on SIGTERM after the counting still waiting for the store. **Trade-off:** during the day, someone holding both the database and `STATS_SECRET` could brute-force which IPv4 address and browser name visited; after midnight nobody can. The database already holds IP addresses (login log, feedback), so that adds little. A cookie or a browser ID was ruled out: in Belgium it needs prior consent and a banner, and Yoshi wants no banner. **Counting:** "Players" (browsers that started a game, once a day, by the same kind of hash) is the headline, next to "Visitors" (opened the page), so bots and link previews only inflate visitors. Not counted: requests with the admin's session (visits, games and the solo log line), prefetch and prerender, bots, GPC and DNT. Per Belgian hour: visitors, players, games and minutes (`*_hHH` keys in `daily`). "Different players" counts distinct names in the login log over the period (together, and alone as typed), the admin's names left out; only counts leave the server. Several days add up each day's count, labelled "once a day each". **Admin page:** a period switch (today, yesterday, 7 days, 30 days, remembered) that every number follows, each compared with the period before (today with yesterday until the same time, from the hours); ten headline numbers; "When people play" by the hour; 30 days of bars with the period lit and a tooltip per day (a tap on a phone); "How we count" behind an info toggle. The tabs scroll sideways with a fade at the edge, the tab bar sticks, the address's hash picks the tab (#logins), back and forward work, and the Scoreboard link shows on phones. Old days without hours or players show "–" or an empty hour chart with a note. Tested with unit checks on the file store and on Postgres 17 SQL (PGlite), and end to end in headless Chromium at 390, 768 and 1440 px. |
| 2026-11-01 | **A name is needed to play, alone too** | Yoshi asked for the name to be mandatory in solo play as well. The start buttons now refuse an empty name, or only spaces, before anything loads. The name field is outlined and gets the keyboard focus, and the message says "Type your name first." That is the same as joining together already did. A returning player's saved name fills the field, so for them nothing changes. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Multiplayer checked for the meetup: silent pages are let go, a full house says so, party games survive a dropped connection** | A QA pass over every multiplayer mode before families play together, with bots against a local server, real pages in headless Chromium (laptop and phone, touch, controller, both languages) and 16 players at once (about 2% of a core, 18 KB/s per page). **Changed.** A page that sends nothing for 30 seconds (a tablet asleep, Wi-Fi gone) is closed. Before, it kept its place in the house, its car and its role as "it" for many minutes. It reconnects by itself when it wakes, back where it was. The admin always gets in, up to 16, because the limit is raised and players are kicked from inside the game. Joining a full house says so, after the PIN check. A page coming back to a full house shows "The house is full" and keeps trying. A player who drops out of a party game and comes back keeps their part: their team, their time as "it", hiding or seeking. Only newcomers seek or join the smaller team. Tag is won by someone still there. Shots are sent on at most at the weapon's rate, and only from someone who can fire. New round during a party game moves nobody. A connection ended for good, or a kick, puts the page back to playing alone with the reason at the top. Names like "constructor" score like any other. **Left for Yoshi.** Everyone at a venue shares one public address. Ten mistyped PINs there lock PIN joins and the admin's login for 15 minutes. One kick locks PIN joins for 10 minutes. |
| 2026-11-01 | **An invisible edge round the built-up area, a smaller one round the two houses during a game, and a ceiling** (Yoshi's request) | Nobody should roam the empty fields for ever. `tools/bounds.py` grows a band of 50 m round every road's centre line and 40 m round every building (the house and its plot, the bungalow, the neighbours, the village, the street models' houses), cut to the area the world is built in. It adds 30 m round every portal, spawn, parking spot, horse walk, cat, clown and weapon, and 15 m round every building even past that area, so the end house of Molenhoek keeps its garden. Gaps under 60 m are joined, holes are filled and the corners are rounded (12 m). The result: 211 points, 1.51 km², every portal and spawn at least 31 m in (the tool stops below 15 m) and no drawn street cut. During a game in the house (hide and seek, tag, the treasure hunt and house against bungalow, whose teams start at their houses) a second outline holds: 22 m round the house and its plot, 20 m round the bungalow and 14 m either side of the road between them. Flying stops 120 m above the ground. From there the whole village is in view. Walking and flying slide along the edge like along a wall. Cars and horses stop at it, a car with a thud. Grenades and rockets fly over it. Pushing against it shows "Verder kan je niet." for 2.5 s; during a game it says "Tijdens het spel blijf je bij het huis." Portals and chat trips are refused during a game, on the page and on the server. The big map draws the edge as a dashed line. No shimmer: at the edge your eye is 25 cm from it, so it would cover the screen on a GPU that is already pixel bound. **Security (position spoofing, "teleport hacks"):** the server no longer trusts a position inside its rectangle. Every state is first cut to the movement budget, then a point outside the edge goes to the nearest point 30 cm inside it, and no higher than the ceiling. Each tick also puts anyone still outside inside (a game began while they were elsewhere; their car or horse stays). A changed page can't stand, drive, ride or fly outside the game for the others, nor reach a pickup, a hit or a portal from there. The check is a lookup in a grid of 24 m squares, so a point well inside costs one array read. Measured: under 0.005 ms a frame in the browser at the edge, admin turbo included; 4 µs a state and 0.3 µs a tick for 16 players on the server. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Meetup fixes: PIN lockouts that spare a venue, party games at the house, who is it on screen, 16 players** (Yoshi asked for all the QA proposals; six security review passes on the lockouts) | **Wrong PINs and kicks.** At a meetup everyone shares one Wi-Fi, so one address. The counts guard against online PIN brute force without a lockout DoS of the venue. Per browser (a signed guest cookie, set with any join answer): 5 wrong in 15 minutes, then only that browser waits. Per address, whatever the cookies: 10 wrong in 5 minutes pause it for 2 minutes, and an hourly cap of 30 (60 with 6+ digits). Overall: every wrong PIN counts towards 30 an hour (100 with 6+ digits), but the cap trips only with wrong PINs from 2 or more addresses, so one address never trips it alone and no group of addresses gets past it. A trip holds back only browsers that never joined; browsers that joined with the current PIN (the server's own list, from successful joins only, rebuilt from the stored sessions after a restart) and the admin's password login always work. Average time to guess: a 4-digit PIN about 7 days, 5 digits about 70 days, 6 digits about 7 months. A kick keeps out the name, every browser the player joined with, and at their address every browser that hadn't joined there before (kick evasion by identity reset: a private window, cleared cookies or a cookie fetched ahead don't help) for 10 minutes; the rest of the venue goes on joining. The browser's id is stored with the session, so this holds after a restart. A new PIN starts every count again and forgets the known browsers. The admin tab has "Lift lockouts", and every pause or cap that trips shows in the admin page's log. The "knew the PIN" cookie is no longer set. **Left as a risk:** someone with 2 or more addresses can stop new browsers from joining for up to an hour. Players who joined before and the admin are not affected, and the admin can lift it at once. **Party games.** Hide and seek, tag and the treasure hunt start with everyone spread round the house's outside places (open ground, inside the game's edge, clear of the cars). A seeker or "it" whose connection drops has 15 seconds to come back. "It" and the seekers wear a coloured ring and "Tikker" or "Zoeker" for everyone. Coins are bigger with a glint and show on the minimap and the big map. The teams game tells explorers how to take part, its scoreboard names the teams' goal, a fresh round follows it, and with Paintball its texts say "25 keer geraakt". The menu opens at the team picker the first time during the teams game. **Also.** 16 players at once to start with (a saved 4 is raised once). New round sits apart from the games and stopping a game is asked again in the page. Doors go on at most five a second per player (message flood). On a phone the blindfold covers the chat. While the connection is down the game's bar greys and stops. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Furniture away from the house shows and stays where it's put; refusals are said; rule switches never show a state the server didn't get** | Yoshi reported that placing furniture together did nothing, and that child-friendly for everyone didn't reach a player. Locally both worked, so the live log was read. **Furniture:** all pieces hung in the house's group, which hides from far off. A piece placed at the church, which is the default start place, was therefore placed but never shown. The server also pulled every piece back to within 60 m of the house. Pieces away from the house and the bungalow now go in their own group, which is always drawn. The server accepts them anywhere inside the edge of the game. It now tells the player when it refuses one: planner off, outside the edge, or full at 300 pieces. Before, those refusals were silent. **Rules:** the live log shows no switch of child-friendly for everyone in Yoshi's session, so the server never got it. A switch flipped while the connection is briefly down (a tab woken from sleep, reconnecting) used to stay on, with nothing sent. Now it springs back and says "Even niet verbonden." With the server's rule on, a player together does play with paint, whether they joined before or after it was switched: tested both ways. |
| 2026-11-01 | **The end house of the new terrace at Molenhoek can be walked into (Yoshi's request)** | Two floors. The layout, furniture and finishes are part of the inside: see the private repository. The street model has this house's side and back windows, with see-through glass in every opening of this house; the front stays as it was. The ground floor lies at the foot of the front, so the pavement is a step higher than the hall: raising the house would change the outside. The inside is its own small model (its tool is in the private repository, drawn by `world/walkIn.ts`), loaded with the street model and drawn only near the house. Nearly all of it is one mesh with its patterns drawn in the shader: no textures, no sun inside, its depth drawn first so each pixel is shaded once. Alone the doors stay shut and the glass holds, as at the main house and the bungalow. Leaving the others from inside puts you at the End of Molenhoek portal. Doors and panes can now be added after the start; a state the server sent for them before is kept until they come. |
| 2026-11-01 | **The admin places Teslas with the furniture planner: real cars to drive, at most 12, kept like furniture** (Yoshi's request) | Ten Teslas close the planner's list: the Model Y and the Model 3, each in white, black, grey, blue and red. They are the game's two models in their own paint. A placed Tesla is a car like the four of the game. E gets you in, with one driver and one passenger, and the server decides who drives. The driver goes through the same movement budget and edge checks as in the other cars. In multiplayer only the admin places, moves (right click), turns and removes (X) them, and never one somebody sits in. Players see one greyed-out line per model with "Only the admin can place Teslas." Alone anyone may, and they are kept in this browser like solo furniture (`briel.cars`). **Where one may stand.** The planner shows the spot green or red. The server checks it again with the same rules (`shared/parking.mjs`). Inside the edge of the game. Off every portal pad and the spots where people and cars come out of it. On the ground, not on a wall, a roof or upstairs. Outdoors, not over a floor of the house or the bungalow. Clear of every wall, hedge, fence and house: the server runs the page's own box test over the house data, the village, the street models' walls and the village's parked cars. Not on another car or a player. The page also keeps clear of the gardens' small things, furniture and bales, which the server doesn't know. **Kept.** A `placed_cars` table in Postgres, a `cars` list in the local file. After a restart each one stands where it was put, as the four go back to their parking spots. Remove all furniture and Reset furniture take them away for everyone. Whoever sits in one gets out by its door first. The four of the game always stay. **Security.** Broken access control (a player sending the admin's message): the server checks the role on every place, move and removal. Parameter tampering: the kind must be in the catalogue, the four numbers must be finite numbers (no strings, no null), and they are rounded. Position spoofing: a hand-written spot gets no further than the planner would. Resource exhaustion: at most 12, counting the ones still being written, and one change every 400 ms per player on top of the flood guard, because each change is a database write. Race on a car in use: one somebody sits in can't be moved or removed, and a clear gets them out first. Prototype pollution: ids and kinds are looked up with `Object.hasOwn`. SQL injection: parameterised queries only. **Cost.** A car is about 33 draw calls. The cars' small parts and the wheels' insides no longer cast shadows, the four of the game included. The body, glass, trim, seats and tyres still do. That is 15 fewer draw calls a car. Measured with 12 placed by the house, all in view from above the lane, A/B in one page (five pairs of 120 frames, medians). On Low (704 × 440 pixels) a frame takes 14.2 ms with them and 11.8 ms without: +2.4 ms. On Medium (1024 × 640) it is 19.6 ms against 17.5 ms: +2.1 ms. They add 427 draw calls and 0.78 million triangles. With every part casting a shadow it was 609 draw calls and 0.91 million triangles. Low cost the same then (+2.5 ms), as its GPU is limited by pixels. Medium cost +2.7 ms. A car beyond 250 m isn't drawn. |

| 2026-11-01 | **The other players' figures: one draw each, the helmet in the player's colour, feet that keep step, and real crouch, jump, flying and riding poses** (Yoshi's request: a better look, movement and physics for playing together) | **Measured first.** Each figure was 28 draw calls: nine body parts, three gun parts, the scarf and the name, most of them again for the sun's shadow. It was drawn even behind the camera, because an animated body had no bounds. Sixteen figures cost about 3.5 ms a frame on Low, in view or not. The run played at 0.88 of the speed it needed, so running feet slid about 1 m/s. A crouching figure was squashed to three quarters of its height. A jump was the standing pose lifted, and flying was standing in the air. A rider stood on the saddle. The player's colour was a thin scarf, and the name couldn't be read beyond about 9 m. **One draw.** When the model loads, its nine parts become one skinned mesh. Each part takes its colour from an eight-pixel picture per player (suit, gear, skin, visor, helmet). A figure is one draw and one shadow draw, and all figures share one shader. A ball round the body holds every pose, so a figure out of view, or out of the sun's shadow area, costs nothing. A ray tries the body (for paintball's paint) only when it passes through a hit box. Beyond 35 m a figure is posed every second frame, beyond 70 m every fourth. Beyond 50 m its gun isn't drawn, but the muzzle flash still is. Only guns within 14 m cast a shadow. Measured side by side with the old version under the same load: 16 figures in view add 2.25 ms on Low (was 3.56) and 2.87 ms on Medium (was 4.75). Behind the camera they add 0.94 ms on Low (was 3.26). One figure at 3 m costs nothing measurable (was 0.4 ms). The CPU time per figure stays about 35 µs. **Colour.** The helmet is in the player's colour; it replaces the scarf. At 15 m the helmet's colour still reads clearly. The name keeps about a thirtieth of the screen's height from 4.5 m out to 45 m, then shrinks. Its picture is cut to its box, so no see-through pixels are drawn beside it. **Movement.** Walk, run, strafe and run back blend by speed and direction. All of them play at one phase of the stride, which moves on with the ground covered. The planted foot now slides 0.55 m/s running (was 1.01), 0.60 m/s strafing at a run (was 1.47) and 0.15 m/s walking backwards (was 0.31). Walking backwards is the walk played in reverse. Standing, the upper body turns to where they look; past 60° the legs step round. The head looks up and down with them. Crouching lowers the hips 30 cm and bends the legs to reach the feet. The body's hit box goes down with them, as before. In the air the legs tuck up, and a landing harder than a jump dips the knees. Flying, the legs hang back and the body leans into the flight. On a horse the rider sits in the saddle as the horse is drawn, with the feet in the stirrups, and looks round with the upper body. Dying fades into the fall over a sixth of a second. A footstep sounds as each foot comes down. **Ground.** A soft shadow patch lies under each figure. The sun's real-time shadow only covers the house; elsewhere the figures seemed to float. **Crowds.** Players still pass through each other: at a family meetup nobody should be able to block a door. Figures standing in one another are drawn up to 35 cm apart, and a figure you walk into steps aside. Only the drawing moves. Their states and the server's positions stay as sent. **Network.** A late player used to carry on for 120 ms and then stand. On WebSocket a lost packet holds up the ones behind it for about a quarter of a second. A late player now carries on for 100 ms and then slows to a stop over about 100 ms more. In a simulation with the same packets at 2% loss, the worst error is 21 cm (was 39) and 0.7% of frames stall (was 2.1%). A stop overshoots no more than before (5 cm). A teleport still jumps at once (checked with a bot's /location trip). **Server.** A grenade's or rocket's blast measures from 0.65 m above a crouching player's feet (was 0.9 m for everyone). Hits still come from the shooter's page, which a changed page can fake anyway. The hit boxes follow the drawn crouch and the nudge apart, so the shooter hits what they see. No new messages: the poses and the nudge apart are drawing only. |
