| 2026-11-08 | **One hard surface per spot on the ground, so paving, roads and our models' grounds no longer flicker on each other** (Yoshi, at the Sparrenhof's forecourt: "i still see surfaces like these overlapping flickering: i want this better on the entire map") | A check (`tools/dev/groundOverlap.mjs`) samples every surface the game draws facing up near the ground and found 4,629 m² where two lay within 8 cm of each other, about 2,079 m² of it under 1 cm: the café's gravel and setts over the open data's paving, the streets' gravel verges over the carriageway and drives, footpaths over the open data's gravel, the church square's bays over its clinkers, Molenhoek's pavers over the streets in detail, a model's own surfaces over one another. Not polygonOffset, which only hides it near the eye: every surface's triangles are claimed on the plan first (`world/groundLayers.ts`) and each is cut, as it is built, to where nothing that beats it lies. The order: our models' ground, then the streets in detail (plateau and bays, carriageway, pavement, footpath), then the open data (road and clinkers, paving, gravel, track, fields); a street's verge gives way to any paving of the open data (a drive); of one rank the first claimed wins. A model claims only where its ground lies over the grass (under it the grass shows, and the paving over the grass stays); two of the models' own surfaces within 8 cm keep the higher at each spot, cut in the model's geometry with all its attributes, so the baked light still fits. Cut pieces stay on their own plane: no step, no gap. Done on the page at load, the same on every device, so the café's model and its tool stay as they are. After: 32.5 m² in 75 spots (the café's ground just under the grass, under the paving kept over it; small props; a fold at the end of Serskampstraat). | 2026-11-08 | **A subtle aim assist for controller players, against zombies only** (Yoshi: "maybe a subtle aim assist for controller players?", then "Do as you think is best") | A thumb on a stick can't stop on a target as a hand on a mouse can. Two parts, both in `web/src/controls/aimAssist.ts`: (1) **slowdown**: while the crosshair is on or close to a zombie that is up and in sight, the right stick turns the view slower, to half speed on the middle of its body, easing back to full speed at the edge of a cone about a body's width (1 to 6 degrees, wider close by); the sniper rifle only a quarter slower; full strength to 25 m, fading out to nothing at 60 m. It only scales your own stick: with the stick still nothing moves. (2) **a gentle snap** as you start aiming down the sights (L2): over 150 ms the view eases 60 % of the way towards the nearest zombie within 5 degrees of the crosshair (the sniper 2.5), never more than 3.5 degrees (the sniper 1.5), and not through a wall (the collision blocks, `world.blocked`). No tracking afterwards, no firing. Only while the controller is the mode you play with and its stick or buttons were the last look input: a mouse or a touch drag turns it off. Only the zombies' own hit boxes count, so never players, clowns, cats or villagers, and only in the Zombies game (not Paintball or Action against players). A tick box under Controller in Settings, on by default, reached with the D-pad. The cost: a few sums per zombie within 60 m while the stick moves, a line-of-sight check per target at most every 250 ms, one at the start of aiming. **Security**: the page shaping its own input only. No new message and no server change: the server still decides every zombie hit (`zhit`) with its existing checks (the weapon in hand, the range, the fire rate, the pellets per shot), as for a mouse player. Nothing new is stored on the server or logged; the setting stays in this browser with the other settings. |
| 2026-11-08 | **A home's code typed while logged in opens it for the account, for good, until the admin changes the code** (Yoshi: "Also tie the home code logins to users. Once they've entered the home code once while logged in, they always keep access to the home.") | A player logged in to an account (role `player`) who types the right code at the main house, the bungalow or the end house at Molenhoek gets lasting access for that account: a row in a new store table `home_grants` (account name, home, the code's generation, when), in Postgres with a key on `accounts` that deletes it with the account, and in the local file store. Every later session of the account is let in without the code, on any device: the game server's doors, panes and inside (`granted` reads it through the session's key), the inside's own route, and the welcome, which also says which homes the account opens (`accountHomes`), so the page puts a small "Opened with your account" / "Geopend met je account" after the door's prompt. A session of the same account that is already playing is let in at once (`inside` with `account: true`). Guests keep today's 8 hours for their session only; the admin needs nothing. **Revocation, decided**: the grant is keyed to the code's generation, which goes up with every change the admin makes to a home (a new code, a revoke, switching it off or on, a new end date), as the sessions' grants already ended with each of these. So a leaked code that the admin replaces cuts off every account that used it too, and a later code brings nothing back. In /admin the Homes tab lists the accounts with access per home (names as text only) with Remove and Remove all (`DELETE /api/admin/homes/:id/access[/:name]`), which end that access at once, the sessions' too. Deleting an account (by the player, the admin or after two years unused) removes its rows (the store) and what the server holds (`Accounts.onGone`); a new account under the name starts without it. A switched-off account has no session, so no access while off, and has it again once switched on. The privacy policy has a row and a paragraph for it, in English and Dutch. **Security**: *grant theft or transfer between accounts*: the page sends only the code and the home's id; whose access it is comes from the session the server holds, looked up by the session's key (`who`), never from the request, and only for role `player`, so a guest under an account's name, another account or a made-up key gets nothing; rows are written only for an account that exists. *Access after revocation*: the generation check means a row made with an older code never counts, and a change made while a row is being written is caught by reading the session and the generation again after the write (the row is taken back). *A session from before a password change*: `granted` now also requires the session to be live in `auth.sessions`, so the epoch rules (a new password, a switch-off, a kick, a deletion revoke every session at once) take the home with them, the 8-hour session grant included; the account keeps its access for its new session. *Code guessing*: the limits are unchanged (30 tries an hour per sender, 8 per home in ten minutes, the per-home budget and slow lanes, scrypt per check); an account gets no extra tries, tested. *IDOR on the admin list and remove routes*: they sit behind the existing admin checks (`panelAdmin`, so the two-step login when it is on), the remove is a DELETE through the same-origin body check (a cross-site one is refused, tested), the name must match the account name pattern and is used only as a key and a bound SQL value; a player gets 403 on both. Logs say how many were removed, never a name. |
| 2026-11-08 | **The start screen in three steps, guest or account first; an account's perks on its card; a guest's good Zombies run kept for an account made within 10 minutes; the start screen in the new brand** (Yoshi: "think about how to make the user accounts more encouraging on the start screen. I first want people to select if they play as guests or if they want to log in. Then they choose solo or multiplayer. Think about how we can do this better in a user-friendly way."; then "Don't show the top three on the login card."; "Make sure the start screen is super good looking, user friendly, both on desktop and on mobile and on tablet."; and the brand sheet of the Game Night poster) | **How it plays.** One panel, three steps with a 1 · 2 · 3 indicator and Back (○ on a controller): **1. Who are you?** Two cards: *Log in*, with the name, the password and its eye right in the card, what an account gives (your name kept for you, your Zombies runs on the board on the church square, your own Zombies room), "No account yet? Make one" and the phone login (both open the existing account card); and *Play as a guest*, a name and a button, with the honest note that scores aren't kept and an account can be made any time. No other players' names or scores on the start screen. On a narrow screen one card at a time, picked with a switch. With a session already (its cookie) a welcome back takes their place: "Go on as <name>", Switch account, Log out. **2. Alone or together?** Two big cards; with an account Together says "open your Zombies room"; a new guest ticks "I'm 13 or older" under Together (the same check as before: the page asks it, the server's join refuses without it; sign-up asks it in its card). **3.** The game mode (one colour and icon each), Start at, and the start buttons, as before. Logged in and Alone is now possible: the session stays, the game plays alone, and the menu's Together tab offers "Play together as <name>". The last choices are remembered in this browser (`briel.start`: guest or account, alone or together, the controls; the mode and place as before), so each step's likely choice has the focus, and "Play again as before" on step 1 starts in one press. The controller walks every step (src/ui/padNav.ts: the D-pad moves, ✕ presses, ○ goes back, □ the graphics), the keyboard with Tab and Enter, touch targets are 44 px or more; the steps slide in, not with reduced motion. **Keep this run** (`server/keepRun.mjs`): when a Zombies game ends (never a chosen wave) the server keeps each guest's run that reached wave 3, as it recorded it (place, wave, zombies beaten, team size), under the guest's session key, in memory, for 10 minutes. The end screen then says "Make an account to keep this run" and the Together tab shows the run with a Make an account button. A sign-up from that same session takes it onto the board under the new account, once, and the guest session ends (its connection closes, the page reconnects as the account). The guest may keep their own guest name for the account when nobody else holds it. **The look:** the start panel uses the brand's tokens (`--brand-*` with the brand's values as fallbacks, `src/startScreen.css`): the roof over the name, evening blue and sun yellow, Fredoka headings, pills with a pressable edge, a paint splat behind the chosen card, the sunset behind the panel. **Chosen by me:** wave 3 as a "good wave"; Log in is the yellow button and the guest's a blue one, as on the brand sheet; on a phone the login card shows first unless the last visit was a guest; with a session the welcome back stands in step 1's place rather than skipping it, so Switch account and Log out stay one press away. **Security.** Attack classes: *run forgery* (a page claiming a run it didn't play): the run's numbers come only from the server's own Zombies game, never from a message, and only a non-custom game's end offers one. *Run transfer or theft* (taking another guest's run): it is keyed by the session key (a hash of the HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie's random token), read and claimed only by a request carrying that cookie, checked again at the moment of the sign-up against the live session table (a session that ended, was kicked or is forged moves nothing), given once and deleted, dropped on logging out, gone after 10 minutes or a restart. *Session fixation*: a sign-up always gets a fresh random session token (never the guest's), and the guest session is deleted; the cookies keep their __Host- prefix, HttpOnly and SameSite=Strict. *Name squatting*: a guest's own name goes to an account only from that guest's own live session, and only when no other session, sign-up in progress or connection holds it; everything else about signing up stays as strict (rate limits per address and in all, the epoch checks, reserved names, password rules, the age tick). `GET /api/account/keep-run` needs a session and answers only that session's own run. Tests: `server/keepRun.test.mjs`. The privacy policy (EN and NL) says what is kept, why and for how long. |
